## 2. Startup / Onboarding Flow

Packaged startup is an ordered ownership transaction. The launcher prepares the platform UI runtime (or performs the Linux browser-mode probe), acquires the single-instance lock, verifies Git, and validates+bootstraps the embedded managed-repo seed — server preconditions. It removes only identity-proven stale server state and stale runtime ports, starts the lifecycle thread, and waits on `/api/health` at the authoritative port from `data/state/server_port`. Only then is first-run onboarding presented against the live server (before the pywebview shell / Linux browser presentation). The server starts the gateway first; the supervisor/worker pool starts only when provider configuration is structurally sufficient.

Onboarding runs after the gateway because a first-run owner must reach `/api/*`; a gateway without a supervisor is exactly what the readiness predicate produces (no second server/mode/state machine). `ouroboros/launcher_onboarding.py` owns the presentation (readiness decision, setup window, window-lifecycle bridge); the launcher stays process/window orchestrator. Completion reporting a boot-pinned value changed recycles the managed server via the lifecycle loop, not a crash. Neither launcher pre-server normalization nor server boot normalization may CREATE `settings.json`: on a genuinely fresh install its first bytes are the owner's onboarding save; fresh-install proofs are gated on its absence.

`has_startup_ready_provider()` is a structural gate, not a network/credential/entitlement/model/local-process probe. True for any non-empty recognized remote config (OpenRouter, OpenAI, Anthropic, MiniMax, Cloud.ru, an OpenAI-compatible base URL, GigaChat credentials or user/password pair), or any active task-capable local routing flag (`USE_LOCAL_MAIN`, `USE_LOCAL_LIGHT`, `USE_LOCAL_FALLBACK`). `USE_LOCAL_HEAVY` is legacy migration input only and cannot make a runtime startup-ready; `LOCAL_MODEL_SOURCE` alone is insufficient; a routing flag does not prove a live model process. When false, the server marks startup complete without workers so the web UI serves the blocking onboarding overlay; a later settings save hot-starts the supervisor.

One served page. `GET /onboarding` returns `onboarding_template.html` with `settings_setup_contract` bootstrap injected, linking `onboarding.css` + `web/modules/onboarding_wizard.js` as ordinary static assets (an inlined `srcdoc` cannot import them). Desktop setup window opens that URL; blocking overlay frames it; browser owners open directly. `GET /api/onboarding` is the readiness probe: 204 once the gate passes, else the same page; side-effect-free. Flow: providers/access, agents, model slots, review enforcement + initial runtime mode, budget, summary; does not configure context mode. The agents step (after access) explains a three-rung ladder — one API key or local model runs Ouroboros; one agent plan moves delegated subagents + commit/scope review onto that plan; several accounts rotate — beside one static diagram; skippable, owns no input; mounts shared login cards in `full` mode, not `compact` (compact omits the paste-code entry a Claude login needs when its localhost callback cannot complete). Account facts from the shared Claudexor status store; observed facts become the completion payload's `subscriptionsConnected` declaration — a request, never an authority. Provider fields may coexist, rare fields under "More options", visible defaults update from the current provider profile; an Anthropic key typed but not yet saved still reveals the Claude-runtime card without presenting the backend's expected no-key state as an error.

Completion is one HTTP conversation: `POST /api/onboarding/complete` (`gateway/onboarding.py`) replaces the earlier `POST /api/settings` + `POST /api/owner/runtime-mode` pair (mid-write failure left providers saved and runtime mode not). Fixed order: re-prove install-time status server-side (a payload boolean is a request, never authority); validate via the shared setup validator + same structural startup gate; read ONE live agent-account/model snapshot when the payload declares subscriptions connected; compile the install preset; apply ordinary provider normalization FIRST, then structured preset keys; persist settings + next-boot runtime mode + fresh-install safety default + one-shot preset marker + durable completion fact in a single write whose eligibility is re-proved under the settings lock; only then start the supervisor. Install time = three proofs together: onboarding has never completed here (`OUROBOROS_ONBOARDING_COMPLETED_AT`, written by every completion, incl. skipped or subscription-less), no preset generation applied, and no `settings.json` exists yet. `GET /api/onboarding` is a pure read: normalizes what the wizard displays, never persists — a read that creates `settings.json` silently disqualifies both install-time latches. Compatible-model discovery, Claude-runtime status/repair, and local-runtime controls use the ordinary endpoints, not a parallel desktop bridge. No second completion path: neither the settings pair nor a desktop `save_wizard` bridge survives; the setup window's bridge is window lifecycle only. The initial `OUROBOROS_SAFETY_MODE=light` (neither the shared validator nor the generic settings endpoint may author it) is discharged here on the endpoint's own server-side freshness proof. A completion failing AFTER the bytes reach disk reports saved, with the failing stage; 2xx is not completion — only the exact success envelope is (saved runtime mode + restart receipt live in that body); an unparseable body is unknown, not empty. Completion changing a boot-pinned value (runtime-mode baseline): desktop launcher recycles the managed server it owns; framed overlay shows its restart card; plain browser tab shows the wizard's saved-but-restart-required screen. A provider save can start the previously absent supervisor in the current process. The overlay frames the wizard sandboxed with popup permission — without it, the sign-in link (the step's primary action) is blocked silently.

`ouroboros/subscription_install_presets.py` is a pure compiler with two sibling products (task-actor roster, reviewer configuration), not a reviewer/subagent matrix; its Available-subagent projection runs for every eligible completion. Normalized API/local settings alone suffice for API-only and local-only installs; a declared subscription makes `gateway/onboarding.py` read exactly one live Claudexor snapshot before the settings transaction — authority for supported harnesses, exact model ids, enabled accounts, daemon's quota-aware unpinned `next_up`; browser and compiler never re-derive account routing from profile names. Emits one real unpinned `agent_session` row per connected supported harness (Claude, Codex, Cursor, Agy), then credentialed API/local actors: a Light-derived Fast scout, and in the one-session case a distinct effective Main route as an Independent perspective when it differs. Deduplicates identical routes, never a powerset; Agy's automatic row = `gemini-3.7-flash-high`, other discovered Agy models remain editor choices. Reviewer compiler consumes only independently ratified Claude/Codex/Cursor policy; fresh-install slots are `subagent_id` REFERENCES into the roster the preset ships — a seat whose session route matches no task actor mints a `review-<harness>` roster row — while an owner-configured roster stays validate-only with self-contained inline routes. Agy-only setup succeeds with its task actor, leaves existing provider-normalized API/local reviewer defaults intact; mixed Agy+core = same reviewer bytes as the core subset alone. Missing required exact discovery → typed pre-write refusal; persists neither a partial task preset nor a partial reviewer preset.

[типо fix pre-write → pre-write... careful]

`POST /api/onboarding/subagents/preview` runs the same compiler against the open provider/local draft + optional live snapshot, without persisting; the editable result is the value later submitted as `OUROBOROS_SUBAGENTS`. An owner-edited canonical draft is validated and preserved, not regenerated. Completion persists that actor value, the independent reviewer disposition, the preset receipt, completion facts, and other onboarding settings through the existing one-write document-lock/fingerprint boundary; network discovery happens before the lock. A daemon failure keeps the wizard open with the explicit finish-without-agent-defaults path; a read or failed refresh never rewrites saved intent.

[careful — I'm typos. Let me rewritten this cleanly in final.]

Validation is deliberately structural. At least one exposed remote configuration or a local model source is required; local-only setup must also route at least one active lane locally. Main is reuired; Light, Vision, Consciousness, Fallback keep their documented inheritance/empty semantics. Heavy remains readable only for bounded migration into an explicit API actor, absent from active validation/model selection. Review enforcement and runtime mode must be known enum values; budgets finite and positive; MiniMax region closed to its supported values; a Hugging Face local source needs a filename. Credential length is checked only when that field changed in the submitted payload — rechecking an unchanged short legacy value would reject the whole form, incl. the replacement typed elsewhere, making that value impossible to repair.

Closing the desktop setup window without saving is non-fatal: launcher startup continues, the main web surface remains available, `/api/onboarding` still mounts the blocking overlay until structural readiness. Linux browser mode skips the setup window entirely — that same web overlay is the first-run surface. Claude-runtime repair remains optional and fail-soft so a broken Anthropic tooling lane does not prevent an otherwise configured provider from starting.

[typo Anthropic → Anthropic... fix]

Provider readiness and defaulting are separate. With no OpenRouter, legacy OpenAI base, or compatible endpoint, exactly one registered direct provider receives explicit provider-prefixed defaults + migration of untouched shipped/legacy slot values; OpenAI, Anthropic, Cloud.ru, GigaChat, MiniMax each use their own registered defaults. Multiple direct providers remain owner-editable; OpenRouter retains router-style routing. An arbitrary OpenAI-compatible endpoint receives no guessed model ids (no universal safe name); the wizard can fetch `/models`, but the owner must select explicit `openai-compatible::...` routes. For a local-source install with no remote provider, normalization clears only untouched shipped remote Light/Fallback values that would otherwise be unreachable; owner-authored values and explicitly-local slots are preserved — migration of defaults, not a model allowlist, never proof the local server is running.

`scripts/build_repo_bundle.py` creates the packaged seed only from a clean named checkout: requires the release tag matching `VERSION` to be annotated and point at HEAD, proves HEAD reachable from the configured source branch, writes a git bundle of that commit/tags, records schema, application version, source SHA, release tag, bundle hash, managed branch/remote metadata. Launcher validates reuired manifest fields, schema, application-version and release-tag spelling, bundle SHA-256; does not validate a separately enumerated per-file "member set" — clone-time Git verification proves the manifest source object exists and checked-out HEAD equals it.

`ensure_managed_repo()` owns packaged checkout bootstrap. First install clones the bundle into a temporary checkout, verifies+configures the pinned source SHA and managed branches/remotes, moves the completed checkout into `repo/`; an existing legacy non-git directory is archived first. Once a managed git checkout exists, a changed application manifest does not archive or replace its working tree. Bootstrap atomically refreshes managed metadata and the official `managed` remote in place, preserving the local branch tip + owner edits. Ordinary restart performs no automatic network fetch/remote movement; movement to an approved official SHA belongs to the pinned managed-update path in `supervisor/git_ops.checkout_and_reset`, not bootstrap. `origin` remains optional personal persistence, not the official update authority. Bootstrap also creates the initial world profile when absent and seeds launcher-owned native skills without resurrecting an intentionally deleted seed. Dependency installation runs only when checkout/bootstrap metadata changed; its boolean result reaches the launcher — first-boot failure logged loudly but does not brick an install whose dependencies may already be satisfied; server health and the crash fuse decide.

After exit code 42, the launcher refreshes the existing bundle metadata and runs dependency synchronization before starting the edited body. A failed install receives one visible retry after five seconds; a second failure is retained in logs and startup continues under the five-crashes-in-120-seconds fuse. Rationale asymmetric: silently losing pip failure makes a later ImportError inexplicable; refusing every offline restart would break a checkout whose requirements are already present.

Managed supervisor bootstrap is the sole owner of destructive dirty-tree recovery. Before any reset/clean, `supervisor.git_ops` writes a rescue directory containing porcelain status, a binary diff, a stash-created rescue object/ref when possible, copied untracked files with completeness metadata, unpushed-commit evidence, `rescue_meta.json`. An incomplete snapshot blocks `rescue_and_reset`; it is not permission to discard what could not be captured. Normal managed bootstrap then cleans back to the local branch's own HEAD, not `managed/<branch>`.

Managed update is the second user of that machinery, with the opposite failure policy. Every destructive rollback path (orphan watchdog, boot attempt cap, failed smoke, failed re-materialization) shares one choke point in `rollback_managed_update`; boot-resume re-materialization resets the tree on its own. Both take a FRESH rescue before the first destructive command (the pre-update snapshot predates the merge and holds none of the resolver's work). All rescue Git processes are bounded by `OUROBOROS_RESCUE_GIT_TIMEOUT_SEC`, trees terminated on timeout; ordinary Git calls keep their timing; managed rollback remains disclosed fail-open. The rescue hook is FAIL-OPEN by owner decision — an untaken rescue never blocks rollback, it is logged and disclosed — and writes one durable `supervisor.jsonl` line at capture time, BEFORE the destruction; a `git status` that cannot answer counts as dirty. The snapshot understands merges: MERGE_HEAD, unmerged path list, MERGE_MSG recorded best-effort; `git stash create`'s refusal on an unmerged index disclosed; `changes.diff` written as raw bytes with a hardened capture argv/env (no external diff/textconv drivers, no colour, pinned prefixes, no `GIT_DIFF_OPTS`) — the only carrier a resolution stash cannot capture — and deliberately NOT linked to an active evolution transaction. The update transaction carries a pointer to what was rescued, persisted before the first destructive command: a replayed rollback does not duplicate a snapshot; a retry drops the marker and re-rescues the tree it finds; the resolver names the latest rescue directory (with an honest count). Re-materialization re-creates MERGE_HEAD and a dirty tree WITHOUT replaying rescued edits — never read as their return.

An active Evolution transaction or managed-update merge uses `rescue_and_block`: recovery evidence linked to the transaction, tree left intact, Evolution paused rather than erasing partially resolved work. With no such owner, startup uses `rescue_and_reset`. Source/local-development startup skips the managed checkout/reset path, performs only dependency sync + import test. Worker startup checks are diagnostic and warning-only: launcher-management env vars propagate into worker, review, and test subprocesses, so auto-rescue would let an incidental child steal another actor's edits.

`server.py` establishes `OUROBOROS_AGENT_PYTHON` from its actual interpreter immediately after binding the repo import root and before workers or review subprocesses start. Hermetic commit/review preflight uses that handle (then `sys.executable`, then `python3`) so tests run in the environment that contains Ouroboros dependencies; plugin verification is part of that preflight. After the supervisor repo bootstrap, `server.py` re-derives `state.json.current_sha` from `git rev-parse HEAD` when no update/checkout intent is active (`supervisor.state.rederive_current_sha_from_repo`, fail-safe on git errors, `current_sha_rederived` event): git HEAD is the authority for what is running at an ordinary start, so a manual commit plus restart no longer feeds a stale SHA to `/api/state`, ws.js reload-on-SHA, or worker spawn verification.

User process tools have a separate surface-aware resolver. For exact unversioned `python`/`python3` on `run_command`, `run_script`, `start_service`, and run-kind `verify_and_record`, registry pre-dispatch resolves once before deterministic guards so the guard and handler see byte-identical argv. Priority: a reviewed skill environment; backend `python3` for an executor mapping; project `.venv`, otherwise target `PATH`, for external/user work; then the verified agent interpreter for system-repo, task-drive, and artifact surfaces. Absolute or versioned interpreters, shell bodies, and non-Python commands remain literal. Resolution emits secret-free provenance, never silently installs dependencies, fails closed only when a system-owned interpreter cannot be proven.
## 3. Web UI Pages & Buttons

Build-free vanilla-JS SPA (`web/index.html`, shared CSS, `web/modules/*`); no TS/bundler so Ouroboros inspects/edits the running UI without regenerating opaque artifacts. `web/app.js` owns top-level page, Project-panel, mobile-nav state; feature modules own presentation; every listener, timer, observer, stream, request controller, chart, DOM subtree pairs with the lifecycle that created it. Same SPA serves desktop shell, browsers, Docker/web, Linux browser fallback (launcher without usable GUI starts the normal managed server, prints/best-effort opens loopback URL, serves onboarding via blocking web overlay). Browser = owner's app, outside Ouroboros custody; fallback changes presentation only — no second API, onboarding contract, runtime identity, or state owner.

Desktop shell exposes `MainApi` JS bridge (`window.pywebview.api`, `launcher.py`): three native confirmations (runtime mode, reviewed-skill auto-grant, skill key grant), `download_file_to_downloads(url, filename, open_external)`, `open_file_with_default_app(url, filename)`, `open_external_url(url)` (absolute http(s)/mailto; bounded join on detached opener reports settled failure), `save_bytes_to_downloads(filename, b64)`. Both loopback-file methods share one guard: loopback host, exact server port, path allowlist `/api/files/download`, `/api/extensions/...`, `/api/tasks/...`. WebView has no new-window/download delegate, so `ui_helpers.js` installs a shell-only link interceptor (delegated click for `target="_blank"`/download anchors + `window.open` shim) only when the pywebview bridge is present — never in browsers — in BOTH top-level documents (SPA + framed onboarding wizard resolving the bridge from its parent). URL classification: loopback file forms → bridge helpers; other http(s)/mailto → `open_external_url`; `data:`/`blob:` → `save_bytes_to_downloads`. Feature-detected per call (launcher reinstall-only; frontend updates with managed repo): missing/failing `open_external_url` + no file bridge → copy-link-plus-toast; missing `save_bytes_to_downloads` → honest toast; file helpers keep `open_file_with_default_app` → `download_file_to_downloads` → `window.open` skew chain.

One shared WebSocket is created after feature listeners register and the initial complete Project chat-id set is fetched; modules fan frames by typed event + `chat_id`; Projects open no sockets. REST is the recovery/durable-read path — the socket never becomes a second task, queue, Project, review, or settings state machine.

### Navigation and shared UI contracts

Nav exposes Chat (Main), collapsible Projects, Files, Skills, Widgets, Dashboard, Settings; About = Settings sub-tab. `syncNavigationState()` is the single presentation state machine (active page, active Project, Projects expansion, mobile drawer, panel backdrop); toggles must not leave multiple rows active or select a hidden surface. Sidebar/Project panel widths resizable on desktop, stored as owner-local UI preferences, not runtime settings. Each active/deleting Project has a sidebar row with pointer- and keyboard-operable open/rename/delete; backend owns the 80-char name limit + lifecycle truth. Unread Projects sort ahead, then durable activity. Deleting Project non-openable until server publishes authoritative registry state. Narrow screens: explicit drawer; Project chat full-width overlay + backdrop; no gesture-only layer competing with scroll, selection, or software keyboard.

Shared primitives prevent competing contracts — `page_header.js` headers/tab strips; `page_icons.js` nav/header icons; `api_client.js` browser API calls + typed errors; `api_types.js` mirrors contract shapes; `ui_helpers.js` status, safe-field, host-bridge, keyboard menu-lock suppression, and the design-system button `createSystemMessageAction` (both docs install its Alt guard on their own windows); `skill_card_renderer.js` installed-skill cards; `hub_sync.js` the one catalog×listing verdict (install/installed/update/adopt/wait_pr/none + badges) joining catalog with global `/api/extensions` listing by canonical name; `client_surface.js` send-time surface snapshot (raw observables, no device taxonomy) that `chat.js` spreads into each chat frame; `chat_markdown.js` rich-markdown: marked+DOMPurify, chat-local URL policy http(s)/mailto + canonical `/api/files/download` form, KaTeX (`$$`/`\[..\]` display, `\(..\)` inline — single-`$` unsupported), lazy mermaid on first fence, bounded ```` ```chart``` ```` with forced responsive options, enhance/destroy lifecycle whose disposers `chat.js` invokes on bubble removal; libs pinned in `web/vendor/VENDOR-MANIFEST.md`; `log_events.js` event classification, shared technical outcome reducers, one factual task projection for Chat/Logs — task truth only `Working`/`Done`/`Done with warnings`/`Failed`/`Cancelled`; no actions/incidents/notifications; compact headlines never expose raw reason codes; `toast.js`, `masonry.js`, `widget_frame.js`, `widget_job.js`, CSS tokens own notifications, framed-widget bootstrap/lifecycle, bounded widget request/job policy, layout. `task_control_menu.js` owns the stop/hurry dropdown ("Wrap up"/"Hurry up"/"Stop now" — frozen wording; host-attested budget-paused member swaps the working pair for "Resume" beside the stop escalation, `POST /api/tasks/{id}/resume`, refusals surfaced verbatim), shared by Chat live cards + Activity: eligibility gates differ per surface but actions, endpoint bindings (`stop_policy` mapping, stable per-task `request_id` retry), in-flight locking, typed refusals do not; pending cancel collapses the menu to the hard escalation only, dismissing continues the run; "Hurry up" acks via LOCAL toast only — never a chat message; menu is page-owned, viewport-fixed flip/clamp, closes on ancestor/page scroll, resize, or visibility loss, disposes listeners/observer with the body portal. Reason: frontend must not reimplement supervisor, review, marketplace, extension, provider semantics per page.

`confirm_dialog.js::openConfirmDialog` is the one browser-dialog authority: confirm → strict boolean; input → `{confirmed, value}` (empty on cancel); alert → one acknowledgement button. Cancel, Close, backdrop, Escape, supersession by a newer dialog = non-confirmation. Native `window.prompt`/`confirm`/`alert` forbidden in `web/modules` (inconsistent across shells, block the event loop; `prompt` returns `null` in the macOS PyWebView shell). Critical controls act only on the exact confirmed result; Panic's confirm-and-send sequence is one testable operation.

### Chat and Projects

`web/modules/chat.js` owns the canonical timeline, input recall/draft, attachment staging, runtime controls, budget projection, routing annotations, task cards, child cards, WebSocket subscriptions, thread routing, dedupe/insertion order, unread state, reconnect reconciliation. Per-instance `chat_media.js` owns delivered-media/link builders, task-keyed photo/file grouping, player registries, object URLs, dialogs, listeners/timers; `reset()` before a full history rebuild, `destroy()` joins teardown. Every ordinary message = one canonical durable chat row; Project views are lenses over rows + task bindings — conversion creates no second message, unread event, or cost record. Routing acknowledgements live in a sidecar keyed by `client_message_id`, updating the existing owner message without a synthetic assistant bubble.

Ordering: raw numeric timestamps; insertion precedes only strictly-later siblings (equal ts retain arrival order; timestamp-free transients append); typing indicator last. Height mutations use a stable-viewport seam: pre-mutation distance to the live edge ≤48 CSS pixels → follow bottom; else restore the visible message or keyed nested-card/Reviews anchor. Awaited Load-older, reconnect reconciliation, visibility return, cross-instance restoration keep explicit lifecycle handling; native scroll anchoring may assist, not authority. Away-time delivery coalesces into one instance-local activity marker (history/reconnect replay, local interactions, layout-only changes don't set it; bottom/jump-to-latest clears it). Collapsed live cards reserve two-line title/activity bands + compact metadata band; no empty Reviews row or fixed outer height; neutral 32-pixel jump control centered above the composer. Scroll remembered per instance, restored after a Project panel is recreated.

History reconciliation is two-pass: progress/system records first rebuild timestamped card state, cards + user/assistant messages insert chronologically, only then terminal state may seal a card — preventing terminal replay from discarding earlier progress, preserving progress-only + nested-child cards. Live echoes + history rows dedupe on stable identities; reconnect rebuilds bubbles from durable history.

Main receives ordinary dialogue + exactly the two host-stamped Project lifecycle rows — agent-initiated `project_started`, terminal `project_completion_summary` — each with the shared "Open Project" action; all other Project traffic stays in the Project thread. These rows are plain dashboard text: producer strips markdown from the excerpt once (before durable write + live send), history normalizes older rows on read (`chat.jsonl` never rewritten), renderer keeps system rows without `markdown: true` as escaped plain text (except `skill_review`, which keeps its dedicated renderer). Project panels accept only their registered `chat_id`; the complete Project chat-id set is separate from the sidebar summary (early off-list frame never misclassified as Main); a `projects_changed` frame adds the new chat id synchronously before the async state refresh.

Main composer: one-shot Swarm planning, owner Low/Max context choice, file attachment, Send. Swarm places a structural `force_plan` fact on the next ordinary message and disarms after that send — never inferred from keywords. Low/Max uses the dedicated owner endpoint; an auto-derived Low fit never masquerades as an owner-selected Low posture; selecting Max may invoke the exact-route capability acknowledgement when provider metadata cannot prove the requested window. Project panels omit global Restart, Panic, evolution, consciousness, review, budget controls — they belong to the one Ouroboros process.

Files stage via paperclip, pasted images, or drag-and-drop; ≤10 files, 50 MB each, 100 MB total. Staging is local; upload begins only immediately before Send. Attachment messages are refused while offline (queued refs could orphan temp uploads); partial/failed upload deletes temporaries best-effort, keeps the staged batch for retry. Structured attachment metadata lets the gateway expose a native image to a vision-capable route and stage the full set through the shared artifact substrate.

Simple text may appear as a pending local bubble and reconcile against the echoed `client_message_id`; its copy control copies raw text, no timestamp. Documents: durable download URL where available, rebuild from history without persisting base64; images preview in cards, other files a document glyph, audio MIME types render as players; a card opens an explicit Open/Download/Close dialog with open-externally for durable files; desktop keeps the host bridge, browsers the blob-anchor fallback. Photos group by role/task into galleries (open/download/copy); videos use the controller-owned player (scrub, time, speed, repeat, mute, fullscreen). Photos/videos keep base64 in the live frame only; supported media stores under the canonical task artifact root with a content-addressed URL (replay/reconnect/rebuilds restore the bubble). Structured `links` rows render ≤12 independently revalidated HTTP(S) buttons identically live and replay. Supervisor transport owns durable media copies (file-backed media, in-memory screenshots, ephemeral turns, split child drives converge on the canonical data root); unsupported/failed persistence = honest caption row, cannot finalize a task card. A socket write is never durable acceptance: routing + terminal truth come from host receipts, durable chat history, task records, state reconciliation.

Direct in-process + ephemeral decision turns create no supervisor queue records; active state lives in a thread-safe process-local registry (`supervisor.active_activity.DirectActivityRegistry`), exposed via `active_direct_turns` in `GET /api/state` and typed `activity_id`/`client_message_id`/`phase`/`kind` on WebSocket `typing` frames. `GET /api/state` also exposes `active_chat_activities`: direct/ephemeral rows united with ROOT managed queue tasks as `kind="managed_task"`, phase `queued` (PENDING)/`working` (RUNNING)/`finalizing` — a chat created after start hydrates from queue authority. Typing frames from RUNNING roots carry `kind="managed_task"` (subagent/legacy frames stay kind-less); snapshot deletion authority covers exactly the kinds it enumerates.

Status reducer derives header status only from connection + authoritative activity (`Reconnecting...`→`Working...`→`Thinking...`→`Sending...`→`Queued...`→`Online`); terminal failure is a factual result, never a reasonless `Attention`. A local `Sending...` retires only on an authoritative typing frame, a snapshot turn with its `client_message_id`, a durable routing receipt (live `routing_ack` or persisted annotation), the durably recorded user row replayed on reconnect, its turn's conclusion, or `outbound_dropped` eviction — never the live user-row echo or a socket write.

Owner-message continuity is journal-backed: each locally sent owner row is kept (bounded, with routing annotation) until a fetched history response returns the same `client_message_id`; a full rebuild replays what the server returned, then re-renders unconfirmed journal rows — a stale snapshot cannot erase a just-sent message; the durable history row is the retirement authority. Finalization is honest: an early final answer carries `task_phase="finalizing"` (history replay derives the same from the open post-task checkpoint, which withholds `task_terminal_status` while open), the card holds a sticky `Finalizing…`, and `task_cost_finalized` is bookkeeping that never resolves a card. Settled `task_done` is the live fast path; a previously observed root disappearing from a queue-authoritative snapshot → state-refresh fan-out removes activity/cancel authority and starts one single-flight durable task-detail read. Page-wide request generations prevent an older state response undoing a newer projection; request-start timestamps protect live frames.

Two liveness invariants close the stuck-"Working..." class: (1) the history window is lineage-closed — a subagent FINAL row older than the progress recency floor (and not actively running) is emitted without lineage fields, so replay cannot mint an unfinishable parent card (`ouroboros/gateway/history.py`, single strip point); (2) liveness reconciles over the CARD SET — after every snapshot hydration, each connected, unfinished, non-subagent root card the snapshot does not vouch for is finished ONLY by proven durable terminal detail; a card with no durable result honestly keeps its state. Header badge has exactly one writer — the status reducer — with the panel-boot "Online" seed as the sole documented exception.

Task activity collapses into a live task card per root instead of flooding the transcript. `log_events.js` keeps live and grouped task cards on one reducer across Chat and Dashboard Logs. Non-terminal LLM/tool/checkpoint failures remain inspectable timeline facts but never promote the card; only authoritative terminal truth changes status. Unknown Chat event names acquire no severity from keyword substrings (Logs retains diagnostic categorization). A failed child keeps a local `Failed` chip + neutral nested container while its root continues. A card holds a concise latest activity line + expandable chronological timeline; server-truncated rows fetch the complete typed record on demand. The compact card is a navigation/presentation projection, not the full result authority; urgent toast/unread stays confined to explicit facts such as `task_incident`.

Subagents render as child cards keyed by actual child task ids; parents retain lineage references without duplicating final answers; nested children collapse by default. Reviews are not task lineage: a reviewer receipt belongs inside the owning task card; a harness or neutral API mark identifies the delivery channel, never creates a synthetic child or proves execution; the selected `subagent_id`/configured snapshot, requested route, effective engine route/model/account, terminal evidence remain separate facts — dispatch intent must not be redrawn as execution proof. Legacy lane/executor fields remain readable on historical cards only.

The `Reviews` section is a read-only projection over independent domain authorities (Skill history, typed plan-review state, task-acceptance evidence, repository-review records), each retaining its own lifecycle, verdict, enforcement, raw evidence; a row is admitted only with stable review identity, an exact presentation-owner task, typed domain state, exact subject/candidate binding where required; incomplete or unbound review stays on its domain surface. Task-bound Skill Review, plan review, task acceptance are admitted; advisory + commit review remain on their surfaces until a bounded exact projection exists. A lone review-history fact may render an inert `Reviews` owner anchor with no phase, typing indicator, or liveness until canonical activity arrives. The projection never synthesizes a task-wide verdict and changes no routing, status, attention, or enforcement.

Task-bound Skill references fold into groups/attempts by the bounded Chat-history reader before window quotas; plan review + task acceptance hydrate through the task-detail seam. A successful canonical plan-state write appends one empty typed `review_reference` to the bounded progress-history rail (revision/fingerprint only) + emits the same live invalidation; Chat single-flights that seam while task-result `plan_review_state` remains authority. Truncation uses the existing `quota` cause. Duplicate Skill lifecycle acknowledgements are typed `lifecycle_pointer` rows with no task id: they may enrich an exact owner but never mint a card. Opening/closing Reviews belongs only to the user (`docs/DESIGN.md`). `chat_id=0` remains the hidden Skill Review partition, never a Main Chat surface; no review inbox, generic review endpoint, review ledger, or second state machine.

Card cost is sticky task-scope evidence: only frames carrying task accounting status, finality, subtree, reservation, or unknown-cost fields may update it; an unrelated per-call `cost_usd` is never relabelled as the task total. Compact cards render one amount, preferring the complete subtree projection and falling back to the task's own; Logs, Costs, task detail retain breakdowns. A running root's heartbeat may carry the non-final aggregate from the physical-attempt ledger. Precedence unavailable→pending→final; newer evidence wins within a class; costless frames cannot erase a known value; a transient unavailable read cannot overwrite a later measurement. Review rows copy/sum no money; new Skill waves expose exact attempt/slot money only in the lazy exact-job detail via `physical_attempt_v1`; legacy rows stay honestly unattributable.

Cancel appears only on unfinished, unconverted, pooled root cards carrying the host-attested `cancelable=true`; Chat + Activity confirm and call the typed endpoint with `cascade:true` (root + live descendants). The endpoint returns only after teardown or a typed refusal; natural completion wins a race; a missing live task is reconciled from the durable record; an unprovable-dead process stays a visible refusal, never painted Cancelled.

A Main root card may convert to a Project: creates/reuses the Project, gives an owner-facing name without asking for an internal task id, binds the task + its canonical origin message, moves live work onto the Project lane, replaces the Main action with a Project pointer; already-bound follow-ups never get another button. Naming reuses an already coined model title or falls back through the server naming path — the UI invents no second name authority.

New Project dialog supports exactly one source: no folder (research/chat), a fresh managed genesis workspace, an attached existing folder, or a cloned Git URL. Attach uses a server-side directory browser (works in web + Docker). A non-git attached folder is rejected unless the owner explicitly requests attach-snapshot initialization — never silently initialized. Clone failures distinguish missing credentials. Attach/clone/genesis disclose that Project tasks receive read, write, shell access in the chosen folder; provenance is a durable historical fact.

Deleting a Project is lifecycle work, not filesystem deletion: the server fences new admission, cancels/quiesces the subtree, then tombstones the registry entry; the UI may acknowledge that deletion started but never claims early completion. Project id, canonical chat history, bindings, memory, provenance, working folder remain preserved; deleting the row is not permission to erase the owner's repository or the agent's history.

Project unread = durable `visible_revision > project_seen_revision`; only owner-visible assistant/result content, delivered media, or a real incident advances it; ordinary progress/heartbeat don't; opening a panel does not clear unread by itself. The browser paints the exact Project history revision, verifies the panel is visible + connected, then posts that revision as the acknowledgement; the server clamps it to current truth and max-merges with the stored cursor — a stale tab cannot move the cursor backwards or acknowledge future output.

Chat instance resource lifecycle: `ws.on()` registers listeners in insertion-ordered sets and returns a disposer; emission iterates a snapshot (adding/removing during dispatch cannot corrupt neighbor delivery). `destroy()` marks the instance dead, disposes socket subscriptions, removes window/document listeners, disconnects its observer, clears history/header/task timers, drops bounded collections, removes the DOM last; late animation frames, history requests, preference reads, full-result fetches, paint acknowledgements check the destroyed flag.

`app.js` keeps at most one live Project chat instance; closing/switching stashes only scroll intent and destroys it. Narrow exception — unsendable client state (staged `File` objects or an upload in flight): the instance is hidden + marked pending instead of destroyed, reused if the Project reopens, returning to ordinary destroy policy after that work settles. Typed-but-unsent text survives separately in per-thread session storage. Prevents hidden Project rooms from accumulating listeners, repainting, or acknowledging unseen revisions.

Chat + progress logs rotate as one timeline; archived segments remain durable. Interactive history uses bounded, archive-aware readers expanding only far enough for the requested thread's filtered quota; Project history cannot be satisfied by unrelated Main rows. Terminal annotation occurs after the emitted window is chosen; display reads avoid materializing artifacts. The task-event stream performs an archive-aware replay then follows appended bytes, handling rotation + newly discovered children; its terminal event performs the one materializing task read. Children are discovered by a scandir name-diff over the main root's task-results directory. For a subagent, queue insertion + the first durable scheduled-result row form one queue-lock transition: a failed result write removes the still-pending row before assignment (typed rejection); a replay with live/durable custody exits before write-surface provisioning and is rechecked under the lock. A follow tick decodes only result files it has not yet read instead of re-projecting the whole store (disclosed residual: the per-tick directory scan stays proportional to the task-results directory). Purpose: bounded UI latency without treating log rotation as conversation loss or mutating artifact state to render status.

The agent-facing `chat_history` reader uses the same live-plus-rotated timeline and may narrow it by exact provider, account, conversation, thread, actor, inclusive date bounds before applying the count/offset/text-search window; Presence provenance is searchable as structured transport fact.

### Files

Files is a full gateway-backed file manager, not a chat attachment picker: directory navigation, breadcrumbs, filtering, image + sandboxed PDF preview, text preview/editing, explicit binary/unsupported state, create/save/upload/download/open-in-default-app/copy/move/paste/recursive delete; desktop host bridge + web fallback share the same download contract. Unsaved text is guarded on file selection, directory change, page navigation, browser unload; Save is available only for a writable complete text read, while a truncated preview stays read-only. The backend is the path authority: every route resolves its requested path under the configured root; a symlink whose target leaves that root may be listed as a symlink but cannot be read, written, deleted, downloaded, or traversed. UI path strings + disabled buttons are presentation only and never replace resolved-path confinement.

### Skills and Widgets

Skills views: installed, ClawHub, OuroborosHub. Marketplace panes initialize lazily and refresh installed state when revisited; the installed view merges extension truth with the serialized lifecycle queue so install, update, review, dependency work, enable, disable, repair, uninstall, failure stay visible while queued or running.

Installation, deterministic preflight, LLM review, owner grants, dependency readiness, extension loading, enablement, execution are separate lifecycle facts: a fresh executable review does not imply requested keys were granted or dependencies installed, and `enabled=true` does not override a blocked review or load error. Owner attestation, where eligible, skips only the expensive LLM review; deterministic preflight + the normal post-pass dependency/extension reconciliation still run. Repair creates a real constrained managed task visible in Chat. The passive Installed projection neither runs Betterleaks nor claims publication readiness.

Widgets is a separate page because extension UI is an execution surface, not catalogue metadata. It renders only UI tabs registered by reviewed live extensions in three modes: extension-route iframe uses an empty sandbox capability set; declarative widget is rendered by host-owned code from a validated schema; reviewed module widget runs in an opaque-origin `srcdoc` iframe with `allow-scripts` but without `allow-same-origin`. Framed declarations may set a bounded `height` from 320 to 8,192 pixels; a module without `height` starts at the 320-pixel floor and reports its `#root` content height through the nonce-bound bridge, capped by optional module-only `max_height` (default 8,192). Module auto-height: host bootstrap owns vertical viewport overflow — below the ceiling suppress only `overflow-y`; at the ceiling release the rule. Fixed-height modules receive no host overflow rule; legacy route iframes stay explicit-height-only (opaque document unmeasurable by the parent). A framed resize protocol is correct only when measurement + application converge to a fixed point; geometry keys are rejected for declarative renders.

Declarative widgets support forms/actions, status/data/text/code/markdown, tables, tabs, charts, polls, jobs, streams, subscriptions, progress, media, files, maps, calendars, kanban, composition through `group`, `metric`, `callout`; one recursive validator limits the tree to depth 8/256 nodes and reports the exact failing path. Nested interactive components use an explicit id or stable tree path; `subscription.render` stays transitively passive (an incoming event cannot smuggle a new active control tree past validation). Text, attributes, links, media routes, field values are escaped or constrained for their actual sink.

Module widgets get a narrow parent-mediated fetch bridge: iframe denies ambient network + origin authority; the parent accepts only requests to the exact owning extension prefix under `/api/extensions/<skill>/...` through a nonce-bound message exchange. The module bootstrap observes the `#root` edge with `ResizeObserver` + a load measurement, integer-deduplicates + clamps resize messages, receives a nonce-bound dispose that rejects pending fetch promises and disconnects the observer; source loading is bounded and aborted when a mount becomes stale. Chart.js is bundled locally; module/declarative rendering must not depend on a third-party CDN.

A mounted widget owns its timers, abort controllers, chart objects, event streams, polls, jobs, WebSocket handlers through one disposer. Leaving Widgets or forcing a refresh disposes the mounted work, removes framed iframes, aborts host bridge requests, ignores late messages; an async mount finishing after a generation change disposes itself. A later visit may repaint the last good payload + restore bounded session state without a hidden live copy. Job polling keeps its `job_id` across bounded retryable transport/408/429/5xx failures + request timeouts; explicit terminal states stay terminal; a missing/malformed status envelope fails immediately; an in-progress status stays pending bounded by `max_ticks`; interval/tick bounds are the scheduler, not a second polling service. Poll/WebSocket writers use monotonic progress (an older response cannot rewind a newer event). Transient refresh failure preserves the last good widgets. Card order is keyboard- and drag-adjustable owner UI state stored through `/api/ui/preferences`; it never rewrites the extension manifest or changes the review boundary.

### Dashboard

Dashboard groups Logs, Evolution, Costs, Updates, Activity; sub-tabs activate their own loading/refresh policy instead of running every expensive reader while hidden. Charting uses the bundled local Chart.js copy.

Logs merges live WebSocket log frames with bounded REST backfill from events, tools, progress, supervisor logs: chronological order, dedupes overlap with the live stream + reconnect backfills, groups related task events into bounded cards, exposes the raw record on demand, applies shared category/severity/review presentation from `log_events.js`. Clearing the visible panel does not delete the underlying logs.

Activity shows running + pending queue entries, background-consciousness state, scheduled work, with mechanical controls only where authoritative: typed cascade cancellation for a live task, BG start/stop, enable/disable/delete for owner-managed schedules. A schedule reconciled from a skill manifest is shown read-only as managed by that skill.

Costs is a projection of the physical-attempt ledger, distinguishing confirmed, reserved, unresolved upper-bound, unknown/unmetered, open rows, finality; unavailable data renders as unavailable, never `$0`. Breakdowns by model, key, model category, task category remain views over the same ledger. Total budget can hot-apply; the per-task value is a hard cost cap over the whole root tree, not a soft warning. Increasing a cap does not automatically resume work that already finalized or paused.

Evolution shows current evolution + background-consciousness state, campaign objective/progress, queue/failure/budget information, durable history. Starting a campaign uses the shared input dialog: cancellation starts nothing; confirmed empty input selects the backend's default autonomous objective; entered text becomes the objective. Light runtime mode disables self-modifying campaigns.

Updates separates passive status from explicit mutation. Opening the page reads cached/local update + git state without fetching the network; the passive read carries the last check's `checked_at`, the configured `official_repo_url`, and a minimal `update_tx` projection so a re-opened panel sees an assisted resolution in progress. The screen renders through one exported pure verdict (`updates.js::updateVerdict(status, phase)`, unit-pinned by `web/tests/update_verdict.test.js`): durable server state + transient client phase produce a status line, meta hint, fact chips, exactly ONE action button whose label is always the real next continuation — Check for updates / Checking… / Update to X.Y.Z / Updating… / Restarting… / Restart now (degraded case: automatic restart callback failed; posts the ordinary `/restart` command). "Up to date" is claimed only over an actual check result (fresh `check_ok` or the cache-carried timestamp); a failed check keeps its actionable label; unknown backend warning classes surface verbatim. The apply flow verifies the preflight through the shared `verifiedUpdatePlan` helper and confirms BEFORE applying: a clean update restarts the server, a conflicting one starts the reviewed assisted task (model spend disclosed) whose progress lands in chat. The served-SHA decision remains the only page-reload authority: changed code reloads; a same-SHA reconnect preserves in-page state and makes Updates re-read durable update status. Boot-owned `pending_boot_smoke` + `applying_replace` phases keep the synthetic `restarting` until a post-reconnect `update_status_ready` proves boot finalization returned; typed apply-failure facts (reason, blockers, rollback/smoke state, stash note, assisted budget floor) reach the owner.

Recovery is a collapsed section holding the separately confirmed replace action, the "Save recovery point" control (moves only this installation's local `ouroboros-stable` fallback; never publishes or changes the official QA feed — see §8), and ONE restore list where local tags label the commits they point at (`/api/git/log` tags carry their peeled `sha`). The unavailable, divergent, dirty, unsafe, failed-check, rollback, restart-required states all stay visible — as states, never as extra buttons. The sidebar update pill (`update_status.js`) is a pointer to this panel, not a second apply surface. The surface is design-system-migrated (DESIGN.md §8); its rules live inside the `design-system:migrated` region of `web/style.css`.

### Settings and onboarding

Settings tabs: Providers, Secrets, Models, Agents, Behavior, Advanced, About — read as a sequence from credentials to runtime detail. Providers: remote providers, custom compatible endpoints, local runtime entry points, the optional non-loopback network gate. Secrets: known provider/integration secrets, skill-requested keys, owner-defined custom keys without returning stored secret values. Models: model slots + effort lanes. Agents: everything about delegation — one service banner, subscription accounts, review lanes, delegation incl. the mutative permission, per-root/depth limits, subagent path roots. Behavior: context, safety-supervisor coverage, task acceptance, self-evolution, prompt-cache posture. Advanced: process, timeout, local-model, integration, source-control, cleanup; worker count stays there (process capacity, not an agent setting). About: current application/runtime identity.

Each provider card has one compact **Test** action backed by `POST /api/providers/test`: request exactly `{provider_id, overrides?}`, response exactly `{ok, error?}`. Overrides are request-local: an omitted field reads the saved value; an explicitly edited empty field stays empty (compatible card: suppresses the corresponding legacy OpenAI fallback); an unedited field preserves legacy behavior. Draft credentials/endpoints never mutate Settings, process environment, or `LLMClient` caches. Model selection reuses a configured route first, then the maintained main default; only the generic OpenAI-compatible route performs bounded catalogue discovery when neither exists. A configured test sends the literal `Reply OK` as one physically accounted model request capped by `llm_probe.PROVIDER_TEST_MAX_TOKENS=16`, one physical-attempt limit, no retry, fallback, tools, reasoning, web, cache, response-format, or capability-learning path. The card renders only `Testing…`, `Works`, or `Not ready` with one controlled short reason; tooltip notes possible provider charges.

Desktop onboarding + the blocking web overlay are the same served `/onboarding` page — same provider, agents, model, review/runtime, budget, summary steps, same normalization; context mode remains a separate owner setting. Startup readiness is structural: a recognized non-empty remote configuration or a task-capable local-routing flag is sufficient; an agent subscription strengthens but never satisfies the gate alone; credential validity, entitlement, model availability, local-process health remain runtime status. Every host completes through the single `POST /api/onboarding/complete` transaction described in the Startup / Onboarding Flow section — all-or-nothing, install-time agent defaults included.

Accounts is the owner-facing projection of Ouroboros's owned Claudexor daemon; the browser never receives its control token or interprets credentials. Status combines daemon/runtime readiness, login-capable harness discovery, credential profiles, honest vendor-live vs local-session verification, fresh quota windows, optional model discovery. One `/v2/quota` envelope supplies both windows + typed per-subject absences (`quota`, `quota_absences`) — a missing usage reading stays separate from login truth. API-key-only adapters do not acquire fake Login buttons. Only fresh snapshots are eligible for percentages/exhaustion; typed absences read by exact subject; a malformed optional absence list becomes empty rather than breaking the response; redacted absence detail displays as text but never selects semantics.

Accounts group into one card per agent family. Header: family name, aggregate status counting the accounts rotation can actually use — signed-in AND enabled, with all-disabled its own state — a fail-safe "Next up" badge naming who an unpinned run would take (one dual-wire reader: unified engine `accountPools` first, legacy per-harness `next_up` second; unknown kinds render unknown), and that card's own add action. Rows are ONE type on both engine generations: on a UNIFIED engine (server-stamped `unified_accounts` feature fact) every account — migrated defaults included, under the reserved `<harness>-default` registry ids — is a named row with name, an Enabled toggle (the engine's own per-profile PATCH), and Remove; on a LEGACY engine the native pseudo-row keeps the same two-line layout but only its ACTIONS differ — no Remove/toggle, because that engine has no route for either and a dead button would claim an impossible effect. A row's first line is the account + status; `verification=not_run` is neutral unknown while `failed` is an error. Engine reports `availability=unknown` + `verification=not_run` → row says "Login status unknown" and its action re-runs the shared Refresh instead of starting a new sign-in — an auth probe failure is never proof of logout. Second line is muted metadata in human words (humanized quota, a disabled row's own exclusion from rotation, humanized time of last verification). The row appends only the exact subject's typed quota absence: refresh/rate-limit/pacing gaps stay neutral and never offer login; genuine `not_logged_in`/`auth_revoked` stays distinct in words; unknown future reasons degrade to neutral copy; prose detail never selects semantics. Removing an account is a request to the daemon's own credential-profile contract, and the complete deletion receipt is preserved: a refusal stays a refusal, while the exact vendor-owned / left-unchanged / OS-user disposition becomes a successful retained-credential warning rather than a false sign-out claim. One service banner at the top explains a daemon/runtime problem once per facet. Per-facet independence holds on both sides of the wire: `claudexor_accounts.py` fans the catalog, account, quota reads out independently, classifies each on its own, stamps the result into the payload's `reads` block — one refusal does not collapse siblings into the global `unreachable` verdict; the client's shared status store reads that stamp through its one facet reader; only a legacy stamp-less payload is read coarsely.

Connect is link-first and harness-agnostic. A typed disclosure renders the sign-in URL + any one-time code; flows needing a pasted callback keep that optional field visible while active. Engines publish optional-without-default `setupLogin` per exact harness row: `{mode: in_app}` maps an omitted browser request to an omitted setup transport; `{mode: external_terminal}` maps to `client_pty`; malformed present data is a capability gap. Explicit null is ambiguous while the vendor CLI is absent, so support is delegated to the exact pinned engine's typed setup/profile admission and the response is stamped `setup_job_admission`; an omitted transport stays omitted, explicit `client_pty` stays exact. An explicit `client_pty` recovery request remains explicit even when normal mode is `in_app`; Codex pairs that transport with `browser_redirect`; non-Codex requests never acquire `loginFlow`. Only genuine key absence on a legacy engine consults the older global operation signal, disclosed as a compatibility source. Typed `credential_profile_required` + `add_named_account` selects the name-the-account face; unrelated 400/409 and prose never do; a typed duplicate profile is idempotent directly; a generic 409 (`internal_error`, or `http_409` fallback) is idempotent only after the exact harness/profile row is read back. Typed pre-job refusals prove setup custody absent/released; unmarked discovery/transport failures remain unknown. The exact `terminal_transport_unavailable`, `terminal_transport_unsupported`, `terminal_transport_probe_failed`, or `terminal_transport_failed` code + its required action (pre-job) or durable `job.nativeCommand.errorCode` (post-create) offers an explicit external-terminal continuation through the same release guard; no prose selects it. Unsupported/unavailable does not repeat a retry the engine did not offer; probe failure may offer both actions.

If the exact pinned engine answers the synchronous first create with its structural pre-command missing-vendor-binary terminal job, the same owner action also consents to one hidden local install through the exact managed Claudexor CLI + one retry; no message text, harness name, PATH executable, system npm, later poll, or command-bearing `not_supported` job can trigger it. The installer is a hard-timeout new process group, stderr discarded, stdout capped + parsed as exactly one strict JSON success object; success requires Claudexor's post-install proof: absolute non-empty `installedBinary` + non-empty `installedVersion` bounded to 256 characters; exit zero without that proof is refused. A second login refusal is returned, not looped. A newly created explicit `client_pty` job exposes its labelled POSIX-shell or PowerShell attach command immediately in both full + compact cards; ordinary delayed/legacy attach stays collapsed under Advanced; there is no embedded terminal login surface or cmd formatter. Terminal job state + the current account row are reconciled so a stale verification read during login cannot claim failure after the account actually connected.

Account status refresh runs immediately + on visible page/tab activation, never hidden pages. Entering Agents is also an explicit owner action: after the fresh read, an already-provisioned `stale` home is restarted through the wake endpoint; `not_provisioned`, foreign-owned, and repair states remain behind Connect. Background polling stays read-only and never wakes the daemon. Job polling uses one request at a time, begins at the healthy cadence, backs off on consecutive failures, and after ten consecutive failures stops with an honest unconfirmed state: lost contact does not prove either failure or settlement. One transition lock covers Start, Retry, Dismiss. A new login begins only once release of the prior job is proven (`loginReleaseProven`): a terminal snapshot whose termination reason is not `termination_unconfirmed`, a reconciliation that found the setup empty, or a job proven absent by 404/410; a terminal `termination_unconfirmed` snapshot keeps the job fenced; a successful (2xx) cancel response alone is not proof of release; a network or server failure retains the card + job id (dropping it could orphan a still-live job). After each await the handler rechecks whether polling settled the job — a stale cancel continuation cannot overwrite a terminal result.

Review lanes edits one structured reviewer configuration. Each triad, scope, or optional advisory row picks its reviewer from ONE flat select: the Available-subagents roster rows lead as references (facts-first labels), then the inline channels — API delivery or a coding-agent session — followed by its model, optional credential profile, effort. API models use free text with catalogue suggestions; agent-session models come from the selected harness. Saved choices that disappear from discovery remain visible as unavailable rather than silently changing to the first option; capability labels configure nothing; server-returned limits + last effective execution disclose what a saved row actually ran as, incl. capability deltas. An unloaded/unreachable view authors no replacement; a successfully loaded empty triad/scope is sent as shown so backend validation returns the real error. A row pinned to an account discovery no longer lists keeps its pin and is disclosed once, above the rows, as unavailable rather than silently rerouted — and only on the word of a facet actually read: an account pin answers to the `accounts` facet, a model to `catalog`; while that facet is unread or failed the row says the pin was not checked instead of "not in discovery". The all-delegated disclosure is neutral routing information: commit, scope, plan, advisory, and skill review follow their configured rows and wait for subscription capacity rather than falling back to API spend; task acceptance alone retains the owner-approved API/default projection.

**Available subagents** is the single task-actor editor. Its list-level Enabled flag + ≤10 stable rows are the saved `OUROBOROS_SUBAGENTS` intent. The owner sees numbered rows and authors one prose field, Description (`recommended_use`), alongside the structured API-model or Agent-session route, optional effort, optional session account pin. Identity is the stable internal `subagent_id` + route facts derived live from the row; the legacy display `name` is retired — parse accepts and drops it, nothing mints or fabricates one — and a changing visual ordinal never becomes durable identity. The editor shares only neutral route/model/account/status primitives with Review lanes; reviewer quorum, roles, schema stay separate. Empty session pin means Claudexor's compatible-account rotation. A saved route, model, or pin that disappears from discovery remains visible + editable, labeled unavailable or not checked per the exact status facet, never silently rewritten.

Saved intent and live evidence are separate axes: row status distinguishes saved/generated/draft intent from current availability and may show the last actual requested→effective run evidence; a status/catalog/accounts failure annotates the rows but never erases them. Settings GET may offer an unsaved migration/default candidate when no canonical value exists; the editor materializes it only on Save. A late bounded status/preview response may replace a still-clean generated baseline, never an owner-edited draft; unchanged repaint preserves focus/caret. Generic Settings save strictly validates + canonicalizes the already-materialized value before the existing serialized off-event-loop owner transaction; a running task retains its immutable start snapshot and the response says changes apply from the next task.

Mutative subagents use Off, Auto, On: explicit Off/On applies to every acting surface; Auto delegates to runtime mode (Advanced and Pro allow worktree, external-workspace, genesis writers; Light allows only children that build outside the Ouroboros runtime — external workspace + genesis — and keeps a self-worktree child off). Read-only children remain available. The setting is owner-controlled, applies from the next task, and is independent of whether the execution route is API or a coding-agent subscription.

Prompt Cache TTL is one global owner choice: provider default, five minutes, or one hour. It applies to every lane rather than letting task, review, and safety builders drift into conflicting cache horizons. The provider-send finalizer applies the choice only to existing cache markers on compatible Anthropic-family payloads; the UI does not promise cache behavior on providers that manage it implicitly. Takes effect from the next task.

Settings save classifies effects rather than claiming every value became live at once. Hot-apply: total budget, tool timeout (the outer per-call cap reads `settings.json` live), GitHub metadata, update channel, MCP configuration (hot-reconfigured by the save; failed reconfigure = save warning). Retained soft/hard timeout keys are deprecated audited no-ops; the save reports them retired. Next-task: models, credentials, efforts, reviewer/subagent configuration, provider base-URL + region parameters (resolved per call from the task-start environment), per-task cost cap, safety posture, prompt-cache TTL. Restart-required: worker count, bind host, host-service port, local-model runtime, the skills repo path (pooled workers load the extension registry once at spawn), background-consciousness timing; a restart-required save offers a Restart now action over the ordinary owner `/restart` command. A failed task-start settings reload is disclosed as a persisted, chat-visible `task_start_settings_reload_failed` event. Runtime mode + context mode keep dedicated owner paths because generic settings writes must not silently lower authority or cognitive/review posture. Every in-process owner-settings writer holds the shared `settings_document_mutation()` lock across its read/merge/write transaction; the file lock remains a write precondition, not a substitute. Async handlers await request-body parsing only, then move synchronous selection, network, lock, write work to `asyncio.to_thread`, keeping the event loop responsive. Loading reviewer + delegation settings waits no more than the `boundedStatusRefresh` two-second foreground beat for Claudexor; a cold refresh continues and repaints the bound surfaces when it lands; a warm result is adopted immediately. Backend failure vs browser transport failure remain distinct; absence of a successful status read is never evidence that a runtime is healthy.

### Visual verification policy

A visible change is exercised in at least one relevant real consumer flow and the rendered result is inspected with vision; a saved screenshot alone is not verification. Mobile, WebKit, additional browsers, and special viewports are selected from the actual interaction risk rather than imposed as a universal matrix.

No visual-QA runner, endpoint, ledger, or mandatory device matrix is introduced by this policy.
## 4. Server API Endpoints

If `OUROBOROS_NETWORK_PASSWORD` is configured, non-loopback HTTP/WebSocket access requires authentication; loopback bypasses the gate, and `/api/health` plus the middleware-owned login/logout paths stay reachable. Browser sessions use a server-keyed, expiring HttpOnly HMAC cookie; `Secure` is set only under TLS so a plain-HTTP LAN session does not enter a login loop. An unauthenticated WebSocket is closed with code 4401. With no configured password, non-loopback access remains open by explicit operator choice.

The executable browser/CLI route SSOT is `ouroboros/gateway/router.py`; file-browser routes come from `gateway/files.py::file_browser_routes()`. `gateway/contracts.py` is the frozen descriptive envelope and endpoint index mirrored by `web/modules/api_types.js` and parity tests; its `TypedDict` classes do not perform runtime JSON validation. The loopback Host Service is a separate token-authenticated app assembled by `gateway/host_service.py::create_host_service_app`, not another public owner API.

Every `/api/files/*` operation resolves its requested path and refuses the operation when that resolution leaves the configured file root. In-root symlinks remain usable; out-of-root symlinks may be listed with `is_symlink: true` but cannot be read, written, downloaded, deleted, or traversed. The backend check is authoritative regardless of browser path presentation.

| STATIC | `/static/*` | `server.NoCacheStaticFiles` |
| GET | `127.0.0.1:${OUROBOROS_HOST_SERVICE_PORT:-8767}/identity` | `gateway.host_service._api_identity` |
| GET | `127.0.0.1:${OUROBOROS_HOST_SERVICE_PORT:-8767}/tools/schemas` | `gateway.host_service._api_tool_schemas` |
| POST | `127.0.0.1:${OUROBOROS_HOST_SERVICE_PORT:-8767}/chat/allocate-internal` | `gateway.host_service._api_allocate_internal` |
| POST | `127.0.0.1:${OUROBOROS_HOST_SERVICE_PORT:-8767}/chat/inject` | `gateway.host_service._api_chat_inject` |
| POST | `127.0.0.1:${OUROBOROS_HOST_SERVICE_PORT:-8767}/ui/ws-message` | `gateway.host_service._api_ws_message` |
| WS | `127.0.0.1:${OUROBOROS_HOST_SERVICE_PORT:-8767}/events` | `gateway.host_service._ws_events` |

Rationale: `server.py` should own process startup/lifespan/static mounting, while `gateway/*` owns browser-facing HTTP/WS contracts, keeping UI and runtime coupling explicit and testable.

### WebSocket protocol

`/ws` is the live browser delivery channel, not a durable state owner. Queue, task, Project, review, skill, settings, cost, and update modules persist their own truth; REST/history endpoints reconstruct it after reload or disconnection. `gateway/contracts.py` describes the frozen envelope shapes and message-type index for Python/JavaScript parity but is not a runtime parser. `gateway/ws.py` performs the transport checks: incoming text must decode to a JSON object, extension types must parse as an owned namespace, and built-in `chat`/`command` frames must carry a non-empty payload before entering the message bridge.

**Auth.** With the optional network password, auth middleware admits loopback clients directly, accepts a valid expiring session or request credential for non-loopback clients, and closes an unauthenticated WebSocket with code 4401 before `ws_endpoint` accepts it. With no password the socket follows the operator's explicitly open posture. The public socket never receives the Host Service token or the owned Claudexor daemon token.

**One socket.** The browser constructs one socket for the whole SPA. Feature modules subscribe before connection; the initial complete Project chat-id set is fetched before the first open so an early Project frame cannot be mistaken for Main traffic. `ws.on(type, listener)` stores listeners in insertion-ordered sets and returns a disposer; emission uses a listener snapshot (a listener added during dispatch does not receive the current frame, and disposing one cannot skip its neighbor). Every decoded frame first reaches the generic `message` event and then its type-specific event, so Widgets can consume reviewed namespaced events without duplicating the socket.

**Browser `chat` frame** contains the owner text and may add `sender_session_id`, `client_message_id`, `force_plan`, uploaded attachment references, `chat_id`, `project_id`, and `client_surface` — raw sending-surface observables (pywebview bridge presence, ua, viewport, matchMedia booleans, `captured_at`) measured at SEND time because the bridge appears asynchronously after load. The gateway normalizes the payload via the closed-key bounded `client_surface.normalize_client_surface` (unknown keys dropped, strings bounded), stamps host `received_at`, carries it inside `task_metadata`, and persists it as an optional `client_surface` column. Deliberately NOT the `transport` dict (chat-scoped reply routing; the surface fact is per-message provenance). The fact is assembled at its PRODUCER, never inferred at render: non-web bridge ingress gets a host-stamped `{"channel": <source>}` fallback at routing (EXCEPT synthetic A2A chats — negative ids are machine traffic and never wear an owner surface); `/api/command` stamps `{"channel": "api_command"}`; external `/api/tasks`/CLI admissions stamp their caller-declared channel at admission, overwriting any caller-supplied surface metadata; machine producers (scheduled/evolution) stamp nothing. Promotion/steering carry the originating owner turn's fact. The loop injects a surface note only when the sending-surface identity (`client_surface_identity`: pywebview/coarse_pointer/ua/channel — viewport and narrow_layout deliberately excluded, a resize is not a device change) differs from the last one seen, plus one neutral note for the first fact with no baseline; absence is an honest gap. The client generates a message id when absent and uses it to reconcile its pending bubble, echoed canonical user row, annotations, and mailbox retries. A successful `send()` means only that the frame was accepted by the current socket, not that a task was durably admitted.

**Offline queue.** Frames sent while disconnected enter a process-local queue capped at 100 entries; the oldest is dropped when the cap is exceeded. It is flushed in order after reconnect and lost on page reload because it is not a second durable outbox. Attachment messages deliberately set `queue:false` (uploads occur immediately before send). If the socket is unavailable or closes during upload/send, Chat refuses the attachment message, cleans uploaded temporaries best-effort, and retains the browser-staged files for an explicit retry.

**Chat-frame handling.** The gateway validates uploaded filenames as basenames confined under the upload root, exposes the first eligible bounded image as native image content, forwards the complete validated attachment set as task-staging metadata, then calls the local message bridge with the exact thread, Project, sender-session, client-message, and planning facts. The web owner identity remains fixed; `chat_id` selects a thread and cannot mint an external owner identity. If the bridge is not initialized, the socket returns a visible assistant warning rather than accepting the message silently.

**Commands.** A built-in `command` frame carries a slash command and enters the same bridge with rebroadcast disabled; runtime command routing, owner authorization, queue authority, and typed outcomes remain outside the socket module. Main header controls therefore reuse the ordinary command contract for Restart, Panic, review, evolution, and background consciousness. Panic is sent only after the shared dialog returns the strict confirmed boolean. The socket does not infer intent from command-looking prose or implement a parallel command state machine.

**Outbound.** Built-in envelopes include `chat`, `photo`, `video`, `document`, `typing`, `log`, `heartbeat`, `extension_lifecycle`, `message_annotation`, `projects_changed`, `task_named`, and `update_status_ready`. Chat progress may carry additive presentation facts (lineage, role, requested/effective model lane, delegated route, terminal execution evidence, review projection, cancellation eligibility, outcome axes, artifact references, nullable cost/finality); consumers must not infer a missing execution receipt, cost, or task result from the absence of one optional field.

**Thread routing.** Project chat, typing, media, and log frames carry `chat_id`; a Project panel consumes its own thread, while Main admits exactly the two host-stamped Project lifecycle rows (`project_started`, `project_completion_summary`) — all other Project traffic stays in the thread. `projects_changed` carries a new chat id so every tab can extend its fan-out set; if ordering loses the race, the server-stamped `project_thread` marker (set once at the message-bus broadcast choke from an mtime-cached registry lens — never a numeric range, so transport ids such as Telegram stay unstamped) keeps Main off it via `chat_activity.mainThreadAccepts`. Task-scoped LOG events acquire their final chat id at supervisor ingress: `supervisor/log_addressing.py::address_task_event` fills missing lineage from the host-attested RUNNING row, lets the Project binding win (a post-hoc bound task keeps its original chat_id), preserves an explicit event chat_id (0 is the real Skill Review session, never "missing"), and otherwise stamps the task row's chat or the `DirectActivityRegistry` entry; direct/ephemeral turns additionally carry their chat BY VALUE via the turn-scoped `supervisor/workers.py::_TurnEventQueue` at the producer. Addressing is honest — A2A ids are stamped as the row says and the `push_log` choke suppresses A2A frames so machine traffic never reaches the browser; the same addressing runs in the server-process `make_server_log_sink` and, via `address_handler_push`, at every handler owning a suppressed type's explicit push; a genuinely unaddressable event (e.g. `review_model_error`) keeps the legacy chat-0 frame. `message_annotation` updates one canonical owner message without creating another bubble; `task_named` updates a card only where that task already exists. Media/document consumers validate MIME, base64, and download-route shapes before building browser URLs.

**Extension WS.** Traffic is structurally namespaced by `extension_loader.extension_surface_name()` so an extension cannot shadow a built-in type. On each incoming extension frame the gateway resolves the owning skill and reconciles whether its extension is still desired, reviewed, granted, enabled, and live; a missing or failed handler returns a visible log frame. Out-of-process handlers execute in their extension child off the event loop; in-process handlers first record the required execution/cost disclosure.
