A non-`None` result returns as `<request-type>.reply`. Exceptions become typed error log frames rather than terminating the server socket loop.

**Broadcasts.** Server broadcasts snapshot the connected-client list and send to all clients concurrently, so one slow or half-open browser cannot head-of-line-block heartbeat and progress delivery to every other tab. Failed sends remove only the dead clients and append a durable `broadcast_partial_failure` event with message type and client counts; the original domain event remains owned by its durable producer. Restart shutdown closes remaining clients best-effort with code 1012 so they enter the ordinary reconnect path.

**Reconnect.** The browser reconnects with bounded exponential delay, shows the reconnect overlay, and resets the delay after a successful open. A watchdog closes an apparently open connection after 45 seconds without any inbound frame; heartbeat traffic therefore proves stream liveness rather than task progress. One served-SHA decision (`ws.js decide()`: keep / reload-changed / reload-unknown) governs both recovery paths so a transient network drop cannot destroy in-page state; an unversioned `/api/state` (sha empty while `current_sha` is unset) stays on keep when no non-empty SHA was ever remembered (owner-selected default under uncertainty), while a previously-known SHA that disappears or garbles still reloads. A client whose first post-open state fetch never completed still reloads once when a reconnect first reveals a served SHA. If the socket stays down while `/api/state` is healthy, delayed recovery probes consult it without adopting the served SHA; a 200 response whose body is not a parseable object (captive portal or interposed proxy) counts as a failed probe, never as health. An unchanged SHA keeps the page and re-arms the probe; a changed or unproveable SHA reloads. At most one probe is ever in flight per disconnect episode (probes are generation-scoped); after several consecutive healthy probes with the socket still down, one forced reload per disconnect episode remains the fuse for a stale browser runtime, and a probe that resolves after the socket already reopened does nothing. After reconnect the same decision reloads when the served repository SHA changed or can no longer be proven current, ensuring a restarted server does not keep old JavaScript or CSS alive in PyWebView.

**History.** Each Chat instance resyncs archive-aware durable history on `open` and withdraws online/accounting presentation on `close`. Reconnect history deduplication covers overlap between live frames and REST replay; Logs performs the same pattern by merging bounded REST backfill with live frames. Large history parsing runs off the server event loop. Delivery is consequently live plus replay, not a promise that every transient frame is persisted: durable chat rows, task results, queue snapshots, Project revisions, review ledgers, cost ledgers, and lifecycle state remain the recovery authorities.
## 5. Supervisor Loop

`server.py::_run_supervisor()` is the single scheduler for pooled tasks. A healthy tick publishes liveness, rotates the paired chat and progress logs (`rotate_chat_log_if_needed`), checks worker health, drains worker/direct-chat/consciousness events, accepts owner bridge input, enforces deadlines and schedules, runs throttled reconciliation and evolution admission, assigns eligible work, and persists `state/queue_snapshot.json`. Bridge intake precedes timeout/maintenance/evolution/assignment so a slow control-plane step cannot hide a new owner message. Three consecutive loop failures clear supervisor readiness, stop its watchdog generation, and notify the owner.

`PENDING` and `RUNNING`, guarded by `supervisor.queue._queue_lock`, are the live task-lifecycle authority. Admission reserves identity before project/workspace/attachment/routing side effects can duplicate; rejects disabled pool, duplicate task, project deletion, accepted/sealed root, exhausted root budget, incompatible runtime mode; attaches the task contract; preserves stable priority. Assignment runs against the same locked state, skipping reaping slots, budget-paused work, closed project roots, conflicting project writers, and over-capacity/depth tasks. Configured worker count is not available capacity: the truthful value is the assignable idle count after custody, reaping, and admission fences.

`state/queue_snapshot.json` is an atomic recovery/diagnostic projection, not a second scheduler: pending/running rows, acceptance and root-budget fences, worker/reaping state, assignable capacity, pool-disabled reason. Startup restores only a recent snapshot into an empty pending queue; never resurrects RUNNING work. Terminal stays terminal; a task with an active durable cancel intent (or legacy cancel-requested latch) is left for cancellation custody; descendants below an accepted/sealed root finalize cancelled; malformed fence evidence fails closed. Capture copies live containers under the queue lock.

Cancellation is intent-then-custody. Cancel INTENT never rides task status: every ingress — `cancel_task` tool, HTTP single and cascade endpoints, evolution stop, Project deletion, per-descendant cascade mints, boot migration of legacy `cancel_requested` — writes one durable row via `ouroboros/cancel_intents.request_cancel` into the compact locked projection `state/cancel_intents.json` (active only; transitions append forensic `cancel_intent` rows). Every ingress fails CLOSED: a failed intent write refuses that cancel typed (`CANCEL_INTENT_WRITE_FAILED`, HTTP `cancel_intent_write_failed`; corrupt projection → `CANCEL_INTENT_PROJECTION_CORRUPT`/`cancel_intent_projection_corrupt` naming the preserved file) rather than tearing down without a watchdog-replayable fence; a cascade descendant whose mint fails is still cancelled in-sweep, failure typed while the root's open `scope: cascade` intent lets the watchdog replay. The HTTP cascade mints its intent WITH cascade scope; scope is WIDEN-ONLY (single→cascade; narrowing is refused); a cascade over an already-settled root with live descendants still mints the durable cascade coordination intent (`allow_settled_target`) — the watchdog's replay trigger: per-task custody keeps it OPEN while any descendant is live, and only the cascade's no-live postcondition (physical queue/durable liveness, intent excluded) settles it, after the summary is registered owed under `cascade:<root_tid>:<request_id>` (replay dedups; un-owable summary leaves intent open). Timeout reaping is NOT a cancel ingress — the reaper keeps its own custody protocol over the `reaping` marker. `supervisor.task_lifecycle.cancel_task_custody` is the ONE settle owner: claims the intent BEFORE any custody mutation (owner + generation, EXCLUSIVE while alive; refused claim exits `failed` untouched), captures, confirms process death, re-checks the child's REAL settled result — natural completion WINS (keeps result/artifacts/cost; settles as already-settled) — reconciles open delegated runs and ALWAYS re-audits (open runs + pending invocations disclosed regardless), captures workspace artifacts (failed/owed-but-unrunnable capture is `failed`, never `missing`; shared tree carries `attribution: shared_unproven`), writes the settled result with reconstructed-or-honestly-unknown cost (never fabricated $0), registers the terminal answer OWED in the durable outbox (or a typed no-chat handoff), then settles the intent, then publishes `task_done` — a crash between settle and send replays the answer. `parent_decision` is stamped only at that OUTCOME. The secondary settle sites (pre-assignment pending drop, budget-drain `fail_tasks`) hold the SAME claim/generation fence. The supervisor-tick watchdog (`sweep_cancel_intents`, ~20s) re-feeds unclaimed/ABANDONED intents into custody — replaying cascade as cascade — so a lost control or mid-teardown death cannot wedge cancellation; a cascade mints a per-descendant intent so no live descendant is unfenced. Queue restore and pre-assignment consult the projection UNDER the queue lock; the pre-assignment drop follows custody rules (stored status decides; a failed durable write releases the claim, intent stays open). Readers see `cancel_state: "pending"` (+`cancel_reason`) until settle (UI "Cancelling…"; a failed cancel restores the prior phase only on a FETCHED live non-pending detail; steering writes — `steer_task`, mailbox follow-ups, `forward_to_worker` — refused typed, the cancel-pending check precedes attachment staging). `task_done` is validated through the DURABLE result UNCONDITIONALLY for every non-ephemeral event, not the event claim: non-settled status, settled-claim-over-non-settled-row, and BLANK status over a non-settled/absent row are refused as durable lifecycle faults — left to custody when cancellation is pending, else terminalized `failed` typed so the worker slot is never wedged — that synthetic terminal rides the NORMAL dispatch seam (assisted-update orphan watchdog, cooperative-checkpoint hooks); the copy-back exception path neither skips this validation nor synthesizes `completed` (`interrupted` keeps its restore-path exemption). Terminal answers ride one durable delivery seam (`supervisor/terminal_delivery.py`): restart-surviving `delivery_id` dedupe shared with the natural final-answer path; a loud UNREVIEWED salvage (bounded preview + exact omitted count + full-copy receipt) for cancelled/non-retry-reaped tasks (delivered before reap `task_done`; also from the finalize-on-miss lane); one root message with a children digest for a cascade (membership merges the root's durable descendants with this run's sweep outcomes, each line rebuilt from CURRENT durable status); nothing for a retryable reap; routing follows lineage chat. The already-settled fast path and finalize-on-miss run the same delegated-run audit and thread `unreconciled_runs` into miss-lane delivery — a cancel over a dead task with live delegated runs never reads as clean completion.

Stop POLICY is an axis on the same durable intent. Omitted/empty-body cancellation stays legacy synchronous IMMEDIATE teardown. Explicit `stop_policy=finalize_then_cancel` answers 202 with the intent OPEN and runs ONE bounded owner-stop episode (`supervisor/owner_stop.py`): live descendants settle first and feed a bounded child-result projection into the root's final turn; the root gets a deterministic `finalize_now` control whose typed first line (`owner_requested_finalization`) routes to its own loop rail — zero or one tool-less model turn, retained-candidate reuse, terminalizing completed/best-effort under the honest owner reason, never the deadline's `acceptance_bypassed_deadline` falsehood. Held tasks bypass only idle/finalization-grace rails; the earlier explicit deadline and absolute ceiling remain hard axes. Grace budget starts when the loop drains the control (durable `control_drained_at`, first drain wins), under the outer `OWNER_STOP_OUTER_CAP_SEC`, both anchors immutable; expiry/hard-bound/pending root/already-settled feeds ordinary custody. Policy transitions monotonic: an immediate request HARDENS the pending graceful intent, preserves durable cascade scope, revokes an unread finalization control, revalidated at drain; graceful never softens an accepted immediate. Every ingress executes the current durable scope — Stop-now cannot narrow a cascade. A successful graceful root suppresses the redundant cascade summary; Panic bypasses both. The UI projects the pending soft stop via `cancel_state`+`stop_policy` ("Finalizing…"). Beside stopping sits the owner "hurry" control: a typed task-local `kind=hurry` owner-mailbox control (`ouroboros/owner_hurry.py`, `gateway/task_hurry.py`) that skips the next otherwise-eligible acceptance panel typed, zeroes improvement passes, and makes force-plan projection task-locally advisory — NEVER a chat message, settings mutation, or P3/commit/review-gate weakening; attempt-scoped (`task["_attempt"]`) and `retry_reset` strips it on every same-id requeue (reaper timeout and crash requeue alike). These invariants hold for every install configuration class.

The event bus is process-lifetime, not worker-generation-lifetime. Full-pool/single-slot respawns reuse one manager-backed queue; synchronous manager serialization and isolated producer connections avoid corrupt feeder frames and stranded survivors; the manager stays session-custody-tracked. Live-frame publication of persisted rows is exactly-once and process-symmetric: `ouroboros/utils.py::append_jsonl` streams only runtime `logs/*.jsonl` rows (never `chat.jsonl`, state/memory/receipt stores) into the process log sink; each process suppresses types with a dedicated live owner — workers via `WORKER_LOG_SINK_SUPPRESSED_TYPES` (dedicated EVENT_Q siblings), the server via `SERVER_LOG_SINK_SUPPRESSED_TYPES` in `make_server_log_sink` (types whose handler performs the explicit addressed `push_log`, `llm_usage` included). One persisted event → exactly one live frame.

Heartbeat and progress are different evidence: a heartbeat proves a process/loop alive; owner-visible progress and model-usage events prove the task advanced. Fresh descendant progress or queued descendants can keep an orchestrator alive; explicit deadline, absolute ceiling, cancellation, and budget stop remain hard. After the typed finalization episode, timeout handling freezes its decision under the queue lock, removes the task from ordinary assignment, marks the worker `reaping`, and hands kill/join/salvage/retry/respawn to the single off-loop reaper. An orchestrator with live descendants is not blindly retried (that would replay the plan and spawn a competing tree).

No retry or new assignment occupies a timed-out slot until the original process is provably dead. If kill/join cannot establish death, the reaper preserves a low-rank RUNNING result, keeps `reaping`, emits a visible wedged receipt and restart hint, and performs no terminal write, `task_done`, retry, or respawn — intentionally sacrificing one slot rather than racing a replacement. The next supervisor generation reconciles the durable record after old-generation process custody has run.

Unexpected worker death follows a three-way decision: an already-terminal durable result wins and is projected idempotently; a negative process exit code is terminal for every task (replaying usually repeats the failure and burns budget); only an otherwise eligible non-signal crash retries within `QUEUE_MAX_RETRIES`. Repeated busy-worker/all-workers-dead failures trip the crash-storm fence, disable pooled admission, and surface recovery instead of cycling workers indefinitely. Direct chat stays available (not owned by the pooled scheduler).

Startup/throttled maintenance reconcile three residue classes. `process_custody` checks strict PID, start-time, command fingerprint, owner task, session, generation evidence before reaping an owned process. Delegated-run reconciliation applies the same owner-gone reasoning to external harness rows; a once-per-generation backfill re-audits every stored terminal result still disclosing unreconciled delegated runs (only this reverse join heals the stale projection), sharing one custody snapshot, writing/emitting nothing when unchanged; counters stay historical; the `delegate_terminal_reconciliation` envelope (`trigger` + `open_run_ids`) is the current-liveness surface. Task/review/project reconciliation repairs durable records whose producer is gone. These are not command-line-class kill sweeps; one instance never reaps another. A dedicated watchdog observes supervisor-loop liveness and a stuck in-process direct turn; it alerts and requests restart, but cannot unlock another thread's lock or kill work it does not own. Ephemeral owner turns remain a separate responsive lane.

Cooperative project checkpointing has two equivalent quiescence triggers: a host-minted genesis/cooperative tree is checked when its root settles with no live descendants, and again when the last child settles beneath an already-terminal root (essential, because a root-scope budget stop terminalizes the root before its children reach dispatch). Event dispatch detects only after removing the finishing task from RUNNING. The bounded git chain runs on a daemon thread, revalidates quiescence under the queue lock immediately before mutation, using a per-root latch that replays triggers arriving during an in-flight check. Only host-minted roots are eligible; owner-attached folders are never auto-committed; credential-shaped files remain excluded and disclosed; every material success/skip/error gets a durable receipt.

The bridge recognizes `/panic`, `/restart`, `/review`, `/evolve [on|off]`, `/bg [start|stop|status]`, `/status`; all other text enters ordinary agent routing. External transports invoke commands only with positive owner identity and a transport-specific owner-chat binding. Commands reuse runtime-mode, queue, cancellation, and typed-result authority. Chat and progress logs rotate on the same supervisor tick; archive readers preserve the joint timeline. Only explicitly isolated devtool roots may use the §1 rotation sentinel; normal runtime roots never inherit it.
## 6. Agent Core

### Task lifecycle

A user message enters through a reviewed transport, is admitted by the supervisor queue, and runs in `OuroborosAgent`. The root pipeline captures the task contract and immutable context core, executes the LLM/tool loop, preserves a delivery candidate, stores the result and artifacts, emits lifecycle/usage evidence, performs root-only post-task work, and publishes the typed outcome. Queue admission proves only durable async acceptance; completion, objective satisfaction, artifact finality, verification, and review acceptance are separate facts.

**DeliveryCandidate and delivery control.**

- `DeliveryCandidate` is retained before verification/review so a later notice, reviewer failure, deadline, or provider outage cannot erase a useful answer. It carries sticky loop-local provenance that its lineage saw a host-issued delivery-control episode: `_arm_delivery_control` sets the marker; every replacement inherits it, including ordinary acceptance improvements.
- `outcomes.py` combines execution, objective, review, artifact, and child-absorption axes without converting one into another. Verify-before-done receipts and exact artifact references are host-attested evidence; declarations/answer prose are not substitutes.
- A forced exit publishes the best candidate only with its typed rail and evidence-freshness disclosure; lifecycle may stay `completed` while objective/review record best-effort or unaccepted.
- Forced exit with the delivery-control latch armed: the model's one forced answer may legitimately be the protocol object. `loop._resolve_forced_delivery_control` resolves it purely (valid `keep` → retained candidate, valid `replace` → `full_answer`, malformed → retained candidate with typed `delivery_control_degraded`) before suffixes/publication and never re-loops.
- Latch off: a lineage with no prior episode treats exact JSON as ordinary text. In a marked lineage, both ordinary and forced resolvers intercept only recognizable whole-body protocol envelopes — valid `keep`/`replace` resolves; unknown verb, duplicate protocol key, or invalid replacement preserves the retained complete candidate immediately and injects no repair prompt.
- Both latch-gated resolvers strip one whole-body markdown fence (normalization shared with `observability._is_delivery_control_payload`, which keeps its own latch-free salvage semantics) and treat a balanced protocol object at the very END of prose as a protocol attempt, never publishable text.
- Trailing detection is the shared `utils.extract_trailing_json_object` — one forward string-aware O(n) pass, fences peeled, duplicate protocol keys flagged as repair intent, RecursionError-deep body degraded to prose, bounded line-anchor retries after an unbalanced prose brace/quote; the protocol-key judgment stays in `delivery_protocol.parse_delivery_control_body`, so an ordinary trailing JSON object and a nested protocol object remain prose.
- The ordinary resolver takes one repair round then degraded-preserve; the forced resolver preserves with the typed degraded reason.

Three disclosed containment residuals: (1) a control object quoted MID-prose stays prose (Ouroboros legitimately quotes the literal in PR bodies/docs); (2) with the latch OFF the ordinary resolver passes prose ending in a protocol object through as text (test-pinned, protects answers legitimately ending with the quoted literal; the hold/owner-revision branches escalate a control attempt into the armed round); (3) on the FORCED rail a TRUNCATED trailing protocol fragment (cut mid-object, braces never balance) passes through as prose even under an armed latch — a fragment is not a parseable object, and containment deliberately rejects the substring scan (disclosed and test-pinned).

**Child absorption.**

- The child-absorption gate is an action gate: while undispositioned direct children remain, the loop HOLDS the candidate (`child_absorption_or_revision_required`, same family as the skill-lifecycle hold) instead of arming the JSON-only control instruction.
- A post-tool evidence change holds rather than arms while that hold is ACTIVE (before the first reminder no disposition instruction exists, so the ordinary evidence arm applies), so the model never receives disposition-tool and JSON-only instructions in one round. A reconsidered full prose answer may still proceed; a typed keep cannot close the gate.
- After the one bounded reminder the gate forces the best-effort `children_unabsorbed` rail with a CURRENT `id [status] sha256` listing recomputed for the forced prompt and again for the acceptance panel's debt evidence.

**Provider death / transport failure.**

- Provider death is the one forced rail that is NOT a best-effort completion: `_handle_provider_unavailable` salvages the best available text into the result body but stamps `infra_failed`, so the task terminalizes `failed` with typed `provider_unavailable` and the supervisor sends the owner an immediate "provider outage — NOT completed" chat notification on the root's terminal dispatch.
- A waited-out transport outage reaches this rail through its deterministic no-resend branch (`transport_unavailable_no_resend`, keyed on the wait episode's latched cause): same salvage, same `infra_failed`/`provider_unavailable` truth, but no forced-final provider call over a proven-dead egress.
- The rail makes its one forced model call only while a call can still land. When the transport already spent its same-model retry wall (attempt budget or deadline-bounding backoff), `call_llm_with_retry` stamps `_llm_retry_wall_exhausted` on the shared usage dict and `_handle_provider_unavailable` reads it as a third sibling of its `context_overflow` and `provider_outcome_unknown` no-call gates (typed `source="retry_wall_exhausted_no_repay"`; every other forced rail keeps its one call, and the `deadline_local` shape keeps its grace call — the provider there is not proven dead) and ships the salvage DIRECTLY — service finalization, swarm-router short-circuit, status stamping, ordinary candidate packaging, no request at all (the unsent prompt never enters the transcript, so replay cannot read it as an ignored request).
- Marker stamping: the exception handler's ONE shared stop tail (every error class reaching that tail is retry-same-request; permanent refusals already stopped inside `_record_llm_call_error` and leave the wall unspent) and the empty-response path only while the PROVIDER is failing (`finish_reason=None` glitch or transient body error; an ordinary empty answer with `finish_reason="stop"/"length"` is a live provider).
- The marker is CLEARED AT ENTRY of every `call_llm_with_retry` invocation because the primary and each fallback share one usage dict; a transient-exhausted primary whose PERMANENT-failed fallback cleared the marker therefore re-pays one forced primary call — the disclosed residual of a last-invocation bool instead of a route-keyed ledger.
- The one repair path emits the same host control prompt, so it records the episode on the retained candidate before returning `retry`; a successful repair replacement inherits that provenance exactly like an ordinarily armed replacement.

Terminal delivery preserves producer authorship: complete model answers appear as Ouroboros; host-authored incident receipts appear as System; unreviewed intermediate output remains in durable task details.

**Task acceptance.**

Host-enforced task acceptance is a root-owned completion coach, not the P3 commit gate. `off` disables it; in `auto`/`required`, substantive queued, headless, and scheduled roots are eligible; direct chat becomes eligible only after an observable reviewable effect or a typed deliverable/criterion. Pure conversation, ordinary read-only exploration, routing turns, and cognitive-memory updates create no eligibility; child reviews are advisory evidence superseded by the root decision.

- Before an eligible panel, `supervisor/task_lifecycle.py` closes subtask admission under the queue lock and `task_status.find_child_tasks` proves the recursive subtree terminal and quiescent; revision reopens the fence, terminal/degraded completion seals it.
- The reviewer packet preserves verbatim owner directives, the full contract and criteria, canonical deliverable identity and aliases, terminal child state, verification receipts, artifact/provenance references, and an explicit omissions manifest; a required component that cannot be assembled makes the affected actor `DEGRADED`, never silently dropped to make the prompt fit.
- A paid panel is priced by what the agent actually CHANGED, not by what merely moved. The host buys one panel per PAID IDENTITY — `sha256(candidate_hash + the sorted set of nonempty (obligation_id, disposition, sha256(reason)) tuples)` — so exactly two things mint a new panel: a changed candidate answer, or a new nonempty obligation disposition (an empty reason hashes to nothing and buys nothing, mirroring the commit gate's rebuttal hash). The evidence revision deliberately does NOT price (cosmetic tool calls shift it; it remains stale-packet detection for supersede paths).
- A resubmit with unchanged paid identity replays the recorded verdict for FREE and terminalizes with typed `identical_acceptance_refused` (a BLOCKED objective, keyed on status+reason like the spent-cap case) and must NOT re-enter the improvement capsule; a replayed CLEAN pass is not that case — it terminalizes `accepted` on its own branch.
- Configured slots are independent actors with adaptive quorum. Each actor gets one substantive interaction and ≤2 physical sends on its bound route. Transport status, parse status, semantic verdict, criterion support, model/provider/route, quorum contribution, and binding hashes remain distinct, so an unavailable/malformed response cannot masquerade as a negative judgment.
- `PASS`, `FAIL`, `DEGRADED` are reviewer verdicts; the host-owned completion decision is separately `accepted`, `revision_requested`, or `finalized_unaccepted`, typed by `outcomes.py` and written only by `acceptance_dialogue._set_acceptance_decision`. Only a clean quorum may authorize `accepted`; the agent may add disposition/rationale but cannot overwrite the host decision.
- A clean criterion is evidence-resolved: reviewer `evidence_refs` must be exact members of the packet's enumerable reference vocabulary; a claim id resolves only through `acceptance_support_refs` linked to a passing host receipt for that claim. Agent-supplied, declared-intent, unattested, unknown, and non-resolving sections never certify success; an unresolved reference preserves the actor's transport/parse/semantic record for audit but removes its clean contribution. This total, fail-closed resolver is why the task cannot certify itself by echoing its expected outcome.
- Actionable findings enter the durable obligation dialogue with stable identity: fix, rebut with an evidence-bearing disposition, or ask reviewers to declare the issue unreachable here / a stable disagreement. Re-raises must name an existing obligation id or are disclosed as new findings; a valid rebuttal retires the row, an invalid one reopens it with both positions preserved, and the reviewer's counter-argument (`reviewer_rebuttal_response`) rides into the next panel's catalog so "already answered" is distinguishable from "never answered".
- Each panel also receives the bounded `acceptance_dialogue_history` (round, aggregate signal, dialogue status, vote distribution, new-vs-re-raised counts) held OUTSIDE the hashed evidence so reading it cannot mint a fresh revision or paid binding.
- Termination beyond a clean pass belongs to the reviewers. Their typed `dialogue_status` votes reduce over contributing actors gated by contract validity — a slot whose verdict did not reach the aggregate does not steer the loop. Majority voting is rejected: one contributing reviewer may hold the loop open only WITH MATERIAL — a continue vote counts only when the same response carries a concrete finding or completion coach, otherwise it is disclosed as `continue_without_findings` and abstains. Missing/invalid votes abstain; one well-formed terminal vote ends the dialogue. Zero well-formed votes reduce to typed `inconclusive` (outside the reviewer vocabulary, no authority in either direction → existing degraded/no-capsule/exhaustion terminals).
- The per-panel timing event carries `effective_max_cycles`, `cycles_source` (owner setting vs shipped default), and `total_paid_cycles` from the same ledger the wallet claim counts. Required+Blocking continues until clean acceptance or a real deadline/budget/round/lifecycle/improvement-pass rail; Required+Advisory may publish an honest non-clean result. Pacing reserves time for the first review and sizes later passes from observed duration; the improvement capsule reports the actual verdict, open obligation ids, remaining rails, and next moves.
- Every forced deadline/budget/round rail uses the common terminal recorder. If the task was eligible but no panel ran, the review axis records an eligible bypass with zero runs plus rail-specific trigger and acceptance reason; a pure eligibility probe runs no panel, quiescence, or another model.
- The forced `children_unabsorbed` rail is the one exception: for an acceptance-eligible root with a quiescent subtree it still runs the acceptance panel with the undispositioned-children debt in evidence, and a requested revision terminalizes as `finalized_unaccepted` (`revision_unavailable_on_forced_rail`) because the forced rail cannot loop.
- Root post-task phases use the minimal `root_phase_checkpoint`: startup retries only a durable `pending_once` phase; an indeterminate `running` phase is disclosed as degraded rather than replaying paid work.
- The agent-callable `task_acceptance_review` does not call the panel for an eligible root: it validates/stores claims, checklist items, evidence references, and the optional disposition, then returns `deferred_to_host_acceptance` and `authoritative=false`. Structural eligibility is unchanged; child-task review and `off` mode keep separate behavior.
- Finalization controls are typed owner-mailbox entries, not injected owner prose. The supervisor may request one bounded tool-less answer, salvage the last persisted assistant text, and retain a full canonical copy when a preview would truncate it. A grace episode has one durable control revocable atomically when the task resumes; descendant activity and host-authored narration may spare the task but do not count as its own progress. A process that cannot be killed remains visibly running; custody checks prevent another runtime instance from reaping work it does not own.

Disclosed cancel-lifecycle residuals (deliberately not fixed): a cascade over a tree with NO resolvable lineage chat whose typed handoff-row append ALSO fails still settles; an empty-intent release can add liveness noise to a foreign claim's forensic trail (bounded by generation fences — never a settle/state change); cascade postcondition timing can flake under heavy load (a re-check races a finalizer; the watchdog re-feeds — one retry, never a lost teardown); and cost projection of a task whose delegated runs stayed open may read `cost_usd=0`/`cost_final=true` while a run is still live and spending — the disclosure line names the open runs, and the cost-side single source of truth is the phase-C C2 work.

### Tool capability and execution

`tool_capabilities.py` is the SSOT for core/meta/parallel-safe/stateful-browser/untruncated/capped-result/reviewed-mutative tool classes; `tool_policy.py` picks the initial capability set; `ToolRegistry` stays the execution authority; `loop_tool_execution.py` owns timeouts, concurrency, live evidence, result handling, and mutative ceilings.

- Presets share one built-in name surface; project focus, root policy, runtime mode, task-contract disables, credentials, resources, repair rules, and delegated-child profiles narrow independently — a registered/discoverable tool is not the same as one callable for a target.
- Lazy capability discovery returns an explicit omission or `CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE`, never a silent disappearance. `enable_tools` answers a REGISTERED tool with "hidden by policy: <reason>" (`ToolRegistry.policy_hidden_reason`, same predicates/order as `get_schema_by_name`), never "Not found"; the contract-disabled check precedes registration.
- The swarm-router's `promoted_task_toolset` is one LIVE `top_level_tools` projection with typed `unavailable_builtin_tools`; dynamic extension/MCP tools stay honestly unlisted. Child allowlists are deliberate narrower principals.
- Review output and cognitive artifacts stay outside ordinary result truncation (shared bound `tool_capabilities.tool_result_limit`).
- Outcome classification keeps policy refusal separate from execution failure: `user_files_path_blocked`, `cwd_blocked`, and `artifact_output_undeclared` are typed non-failure/policy-denial surfaces; a declared output that cannot be registered is the genuine `artifact_output_error`. An expected authority boundary never becomes the task's headline failure while real artifact loss is preserved.

#### Web access mechanisms (three distinct paths — do not conflate)

The three paths differ in who chooses the query, which model reasons, which authority fetches, and where evidence is recorded:

1. **Main-loop native search.** A provider server tool attaches to the main solve-model request only when the main-loop setting allows it; the same solve model decides — no second reasoning model. Citations/request counts fold into `llm_usage` and the task's host-attested retrieval fact — context for task acceptance, never a criterion; absence only means this path recorded no search. The provider-side query is not host-available and is not claimed as logged.
2. **`web_search` function tool.** `ToolRegistry` executes a separate search call via the configured web-search route or a keyless retrieval backend; a provider-backed call can introduce a second model and its own cost. Arguments and bounded results land in `tools.jsonl`; they are not native-search usage on the answering call.
3. **Browser tools.** `browse_page` and `browser_action` drive a local stateful Playwright session; arguments and result previews are tool evidence. Browser state and local action semantics are not equivalent to either provider-native path.

For delegated profiles, the URL, route, private-range, and control-plane guards apply to both local-readonly and acting children. JavaScript `evaluate` is exposed only to a valid acting child on its current page; local-readonly execution and schema stay blocked, while the shared owner/self-lowering checks still run for acting evaluation. Acting children retain the shell-to-loopback `/ws` route; this adds no WebSocket authentication and never broadens that route to local-readonly children. This separation is methodological authority: an evaluation or acceptance claim must name the path actually used — provider-native search, a separate search model, and a local browser are not interchangeable.

#### Context fitting, retry, and compaction

- `config.get_context_mode()` is the effective Main sizing/rendering source; `config.get_owner_context_mode()` is the persistent owner-intent/P3 source; they differ only in the auto-Low compatibility window, where bare env Low sizes Main as Low but remains owner Max absent an explicit false-provenance tombstone.
- `context_layout.py` owns the reference-form projection: in Max, `ARCHITECTURE.md` is class-scoped (v6.115.0, owner decision) — full-resident for self-body classes (default-lane pooled tasks, evolution, self-body work) and replaced by its lossless H2-H4 navigation map plus a visible on-demand pointer for direct-chat turns and externally-bound classes (external workspace, project tree, subagent, API/CLI/scheduled surface); in Low it is the navigation map for every class. `DEVELOPMENT.md` inclusion is mode-independent: full when the active repository binding targets Ouroboros's own body, otherwise a visible on-demand pointer (external workspace, project tree, subagent, API/CLI/scheduled surface). Its owner-max rendered form is class-scoped too (v6.116.0, owner decision — the ARCHITECTURE mirror): the inlined handbook renders full for self-body classes and as its lossless H2-H4 navigation map + visible on-demand pointer for direct-chat turns; Low keeps the full form; `context_requires_development` keeps its exact old override semantics (False → pointer-only, never nav). Project membership is not the signal; `workspace="none"` retains the handbook and keeps ARCHITECTURE full (no external binding); evolution and self-body work retain both; `context_requires_development` and `context_requires_self_body_docs` override. Context economy comes from dropping the self-engineering handbook for external work and, in Max, from relocating the capability map and the handbook for classes that never open them — the nav map + named pointer is a lossless relocation, never a silent truncation.
- `context_fit.py` renders Max and Low projections from one immutable core and measures each candidate on one labelled density basis against the selected route capacity. Owner Low has an elastic 200K total-context economy target; crossing it is never a synthetic failure. With unknown capacity, owner Max gets one honest Max call, while owner Low may reclaim toward its known target before best effort.
- Predicted Max pressure retains the Max document projection and may request one deficit-sized mutable-history pass; only an actual provider overflow authorizes task-local Low, after which one same-route semantic recovery is permitted only when the final post-transform candidate has the same route, round, and response reserve and strictly fewer context-bearing bytes. Owner mode and P3 applicability never change; P3 commit/scope review keeps its separate fit and oversize policy.
- `context_compaction.py` is a requested materializer, not a second threshold/timer/retry authority. Pure selection picks a positive-reclaim prefix of completed assistant-call plus contiguous matching-result units; owner turns and malformed, interrupted, or visually opaque units remain verbatim. A non-empty selection first writes an exact private checkpoint, then summarizes complete gap-free hashed map/fold input; independent covered units may apply while a failed unit stays raw. Recompactable host-only capsules retain generation, lineage, and checkpoint/CAS refs and are stripped from physical candidates. Selection and receipts use the caller's exact density plus the same bounded image projection as ContextFit. No eligible positive reclaim means no checkpoint, summarizer call, or transcript mutation; the one route+round latch prevents repeating the same automatic pass without a hysteresis timer.

**Provider-neutral conversation and OpenAI custom-tools ladder.**

- Ouroboros stores one provider-neutral, function-shaped conversation. Direct OpenAI agent/tool traffic stays on Chat Completions: `openai_chat_custom.py` projects only the physical copy into Chat custom tools and normalizes returned custom calls back into canonical function calls.
- For direct OpenAI Chat with tools and a requested non-`none` effort, every eligible call begins with custom and that exact effort; generic parameter repair composes on the current rung before a dialect change. Only an exact custom-dialect rejection advances to a fresh function candidate with the original effort; only an exact function-dialect rejection may advance to explicit `none` (ladder ordinals custom/function/none 1/2/3).
- The custom→function fallback is never learned as a durable dialect action; the last candidate exists only inside the current call under the caller's physical-attempt rail. The compatibility layer never migrates the conversation to Responses, changes the model/provider/API surface, or raises an attempt limit.
- `request_wire_recovery.py` is the single provider-neutral adaptation driver for exception and HTTP-200 body-error shapes, sync and async. It identifies one credential-free exact route/profile (provider, endpoint, API surface, resolved model, reasoning carrier, tool dialect/choice/strictness, relevant-value predicate), applies fresh typed evidence before send, and composes only bounded `set_value`, `drop_field`, and registered `replace_dialect` actions.
- A learnable reactive action stays pending until a semantically valid normalized response is bound to the exact settled physical-attempt capture; only that success writes the 14-day cross-process store. A non-reasoning repair inside an already degraded task-local rung is disclosed task-local and never becomes pending/durable. Provider prose cannot switch routes; legacy model-global effort/rejected-parameter rows are readable diagnostics only.
- Every settled candidate is disclosed as `usage.request_wire`; nested callers aggregate terminal per-call disclosures in order as `request_wire_history` (bounded, explicit omitted count). This is not the physical-attempt ledger — `state/usage_attempts.jsonl` remains the complete monetary/attempt authority. Private custom-argument receipts never enter stored history or public observability.
- Direct Anthropic tool turns retain a private route-bound receipt with the complete native assistant `content` list in original order (thinking/redacted-thinking, `caller`, signatures, future opaque members); the immediately matching same-route continuation replays that list byte-for-byte before its tool results; a provider/endpoint/API-surface/model change scrubs the receipt. An unfinished native unit cannot be compacted; summarizer/public projections omit opaque values, while private checkpoints may retain the receipt for crash recovery. Owner-requested `none` is sent as `thinking.type=disabled`; a successful exact-route repair may disclose provider-default thinking, never a guessed legacy `budget_tokens` mapping.
- Retry budgets are failure-class specific. Empty/incomplete responses and transient 429/5xx/overload failures may retry the same model with deadline-bounded backoff; auth, quota, permanent bad requests, and confirmed oversize fail fast. Same-route request-wire recovery stays under the physical-attempt authority; exhausted compatibility returns to the configured fallback chain rather than becoming a second router. `LLMClient` keeps leading system messages authoritative and demotes later notices to visibly marked user notices while preserving assistant-tool-result adjacency.

**Transport failure classes (physical-attempt authority).**

- A REMOTE pre-dispatch transport failure is its own class, `transport_unavailable` (`loop_llm_call.classify_llm_exception`): released physical-attempt custody plus the typed pre-dispatch predicate plus a non-local provider, exactly ONE physical attempt per call — pacing belongs to the round-level wait episode (`ouroboros/loop_transport.py`).
- The round gate latches an episode, optionally walks the fallback chain once when `USE_LOCAL_FALLBACK` makes it local (a mid-chain pre-dispatch failure stops the walk), then waits: durable `network_wait` events in events.jsonl, owner progress notes on a `min(NETWORK_WAIT_NOTE_INTERVAL_SEC, idle_timeout/2)` cadence (keeping the idle rail alive), an owner-signal-interruptible backoff sleep, and a free redial of the SAME round (round budget not consumed; the round top re-runs message drain, Stop/finalize controls, budget rails, and model overrides).
- The wait is bounded only by existing rails — owner deadline minus the dispatch-admission reserve (with one last free redial before the window closes), budget, Stop, and the supervisor's absolute ceiling — never by a new setting; the acceptance-review percentage reserve is deliberately not a wait ceiling. Direct-chat and ephemeral decision turns never wait; exact-model routes wait and redial their own pinned model with no local substitution.
- When the rails run out, `_handle_provider_unavailable` takes a deterministic no-resend terminal keyed on the episode's latched cause (`transport_unavailable_no_resend`, reason `provider_unavailable`, execution `infra_failed`) — salvage without a forced-final provider call, mirroring `provider_outcome_unknown`. Consequence: `OUROBOROS_TRANSIENT_RETRY_MAX` still bounds same-model transient attempts, but a dead egress holds the task until its deadline/ceiling.
- Mid-flight is `provider_outcome_unknown`: the dispatched request is NEVER resent and stays billed at its reserved upper bound forever (the ledger's `unresolved` state is terminal); review/safety/consciousness actors keep their own bounded send caps.
- The one owner-ratified exception: a configured-session nanny with EXACTLY one live delegated leaf parks in `supervised_wait` via a durable unknown-provider hold (`ouroboros/delegate_hold.py`) instead of dying and cancelling a healthy leaf; a meaningful leaf wake (a unique host-attested receipt, absent from the unknown request) resumes the task with a NEW round, control wakes exit through the unknown no-resend terminal, and a terminal-but-unsettled leaf never enters the hold. Budget admission for wake rounds stays fail-closed against the eternal unresolved upper bound.
- OpenAI-compatible response choices keep their outer `finish_reason` as the bounded per-call usage fact `response_finish_reason` (plus a bounded upstream `response_provider` label when supplied). These are observational fields only: host-owned reserved keys, provider extension values discarded unless the designated outer response field supplies them, never in canonical assistant history, never changing the empty-response classifier or retry policy.
- The trusted provider canary accepts a schema-valid native tool call even with assistant text, recording only length and hash as warning telemetry (visible on a passing CI run); malformed native arguments remain a red contract failure — no prose parser, salvage, provider hop, or unbounded retry; diagnostics omit raw provider content and reasoning payloads.

**Prompt caching.**

- Prompt caching is stable-first. Governance and task-stable contracts precede mutable evidence; review builders disclose the stable/dynamic boundary and keep untrusted payloads outside the governance cache block. Provider-specific cache hints are sent only where supported and receive one exact retry without the rejected hint; rejection evidence is durable and route-specific. Cache identity must not weaken exact review bindings or create a second review authority.
- Gateway response-cache recovery is narrower and reactive. The first call remains cacheable; only a main-loop `provider_incomplete_response` arms a fresh-response request for later attempts. Only the generic `openai-compatible` route (Ouroboros's gateway/proxy route) renders LiteLLM's documented `extra_body.cache.no-cache` control; a strict compatible endpoint that explicitly rejects `cache` receives the one exact cache-parameter removal retry. Direct providers and OpenRouter never receive this field; no URL/model heuristic or parallel gateway-capability subsystem is introduced.
#### Vision and local image evidence

`analyze_screenshot`/`vlm_query` are bounded secondary-model calls through `LLMClient.vision_query`; `view_image` attaches a local image natively to the active conversation. Send-time image routing works on a transcript copy, so captioning/placeholder conversion never mutates canonical history. Image payloads are validated, capped/downscaled, and confined to readable roots from the Tool API policy matrix plus the protected-artifact rule; URLs and inline base64 are not accepted as local paths. `vision.attach_local_image_to_context` is the single attachment seam for an explicit `view_image` call and a tool result carrying the typed `auto_attach_image` opt-in: same durable copy, trust boundary, and live-image eviction budget; auto-attach failure is non-fatal (result and path stay visible). Vision/local-media tools are not web tools and may be withheld by the task contract.

#### Background consciousness and Evolution

BG Consciousness is the high-horizon awareness loop: it grooms memory, identity candidates, knowledge, and the improvement backlog; messages the owner; may initiate an already-configured reviewed Presence binding — without directly acquiring its tools/transport authority or running shell/code, subagents, reviews, commits, or evolution toggles. Evolution Campaigns are ordinary governed tasks, so awareness proposes without bypassing task, budget, and review authority.

Inbox: append-only `state/consciousness_observations.jsonl`. Producers write a stable-ID `enqueue` row before the wake notification returns; `_snapshot_pending_observations` reads a cached, insertion-ordered projection; `_ack_observations` appends ACK rows only after the thought receipt, tool receipts, budget settlement, and other durable writes succeed. Provider error, overflow, cancellation, unreadable/malformed row, or unknown ACK leaves the snapshot pending and exposes the actor-readable source (`read_file(root='runtime_data', path='state/consciousness_observations.jsonl')`). Status reports only pending count, oldest timestamp, source, and gap count — never treating the bounded projection as the observation source. If the same cycle calls `update_identity`, an observation gap joins the identity-completeness envelope and blocks that destructive write until the actor resolves the named source; direct BGC autonomy is preserved.

A campaign owns an explicit objective, campaign id, transaction, and task claim; `evolution_mode_enabled` is only its scheduling projection. Dispatch, review, commit, publication, and restart revalidate that authority; a restored row without a live uncommitted claim is cancelled. A reviewed commit binds to the claim by exact SHA before publication; if authority changes after commit, the commit moves to a private inspection ref and any attempt-created tag is removed from the normal namespace (no reset of concurrent index/worktree edits, no restart). Restart verification and boot reconciliation decide absorbed/abandoned/pending; exact terminal replay resumes only incomplete effects, never double-counting a cycle.

Cleanup is deterministic and custody-aware. A no-op/abandoned cycle may restore the transaction base while preserving dirty/ahead work in recorded stash or local refs, but cleanup is skipped when another task, a live test, or the operator kill-switch makes reset unsafe. A byte-identical diff whose last terminal is a review-verdict block is refused free from the first block (preflight failures and infra-blocks neither build nor reset the streak); changing the diff, or a rebuttal whose content hash is new to the streak, creates a new paid reviewable case; the shared cycle cap bounds paid triad+scope cycles per root. Checkpoint/outcome rows preserve git/memory identity, cost, rounds, and explicit omissions. Agent-requested restart drains heartbeat-fresh running tasks up to its configured bound, then fails closed rather than cutting another task silently.

Post-task evolution is owner-gated and default-off: the worker may recommend one backlog item by writing a request; only the supervisor converts it into one normal campaign cycle. An owner stop closes the campaign, clears queued requests, persists a sentinel, and cannot be autonomously reversed; only an owner-authorized start clears it. Evolution is hard-blocked in `light` runtime mode at every entry point; `advanced`/`pro` use the normal task/review path.

Loop self-checkpoints remain plain user-message reminders — deliberately no second structured, tool-less reflection protocol in the hot loop (strict parsing and prompt-shape changes previously produced unusable records and destroyed cache continuity); durable learning belongs to the post-task reflection flow.

Tool API v2 exposes neutral canonical names directly: `read_file`, `list_files`, `search_code`, `write_file`, `edit_text`, `edit_batch`, `apply_patch`, `run_command`, `run_script`, `verify_and_record`, service tools, `commit_reviewed`, `vcs_*`, `schedule_subagent`, `schedule_followup`, `wait_task`, `wait_tasks`. Legacy public tool names are not exposed and not translated at execute time.

File tools share a path-based public ABI: `list_files` uses `path` like `read_file`/`write_file`/`edit_text`/`search_code`, not a separate `dir`; `edit_batch` carries `path` inside each `edits[]` entry; `apply_patch` addresses files inside the patch text (`*** Update File: <path>`), same root-aware resolution. Because payload-riding paths miss the dispatch seam that rewrites a `path` ARG (`_PATH_NORMALIZED_TOOLS`), both ends canonicalize explicitly via `tool_access.canonical_repo_relative_path`: the handler before its protected checks, and the dispatch gates via `_payload_write_paths` (reads apply_patch's targets back out of the real parser). One normalization contract keeps a guard from judging `repo/BIBLE.md` while the write lands on `BIBLE.md`; `_ROOT_ARG_REPO_WRITE_TOOLS` is the single set every repo-write fence keys on.

Filesystem tool output is self-locating: file/search/edit/write results use canonical `root:path` labels; `run_command`/`run_script` echo the resolved `cwd` in result headers — root mismatches stay visible without collapsing storage or safety boundaries. `user_files` is the first-class root for user-visible files under the owner's home (relative home paths `Desktop/report.html`, `~` paths, safe absolute home paths; the Ouroboros repo and runtime control-plane are rejected). `task_drive` is task-scoped scratch; `artifact_store` is task-scoped under `data/task_results/artifacts/<task_id>/`; external deliverables via `user_files` or declared process `outputs` are copied into that canonical store for audit (declared directory outputs as bounded manifest+zip pairs). A rewritten user-visible file keeps its previous copy under `task_results/artifact_versions/<task_id>/` (last-5 retention), recoverable but not advertised. Two READ-ONLY orchestrator roots complete the set: `subagent_projects` and `deliverables` grant read/list/search only to orchestrator profiles (never write/shell/cwd, never handed to a subagent); the logical `deliverables` root stays read-only — a top-level task writes the separate physical Deliverables container via `user_files`-authorized paths (narrow shell seam, declared `outputs`, undeclared-output diagnostic/custody flow). That audit is best-effort (deferred residuals: `cd`-relative writes, shell-variable/indirect destinations, inline-code path construction). Direct `cp`/`mv`/`ln` directory destinations derive their immediate child target (`-tDIR`/`-Ssuffix`, `cp --parents`, `cp -s`); `ln --relative` resolves its source from the command cwd; recursive copies are not walked for nested symlinks or hidden descendants. For argv-visible targets the shell guard checks the lexical Deliverables origin before generic workspace/executor roots, then the symlink-resolved destination, so hidden/credential-like/protected/symlink-escaping descendants never inherit broader admission; target-first also applies to declared-output custody and Presence ceilings (logical `user_files`-relative prefix when the physical Deliverables binding remaps). Declared outputs still use normal custody. Inode aliases (hardlinks) are a disclosed filesystem residual.
### Safety and runtime mode

Every tool call crosses deterministic `ToolRegistry` + resource-root guards; policy LLM safety (`safety.py`) is added per `OUROBOROS_SAFETY_MODE`; deterministic layers run in every mode. Safety-model context is bounded by `_SAFETY_CONTEXT_CHAR_BUDGET` (newest-first, count-bearing omission marker reserved inside the budget — never a silent cut). Rate-limited safety past its one bounded retry → guarded call refused with typed non-verdict `⚠️ SAFETY_UNAVAILABLE` (a plain tool error downstream, never `safety_violation`) plus durable audit; an unchecked guarded call is never executed and never accused (the documented local-FALLBACK lane fails open with an audited `SAFETY_WARNING`). `runtime_mode_policy.py` owns protected self-repo paths, frozen contracts, release/build, and managed-repo invariants. Light blocks Ouroboros self-repo/control-plane mutation (`LIGHT_MODE_REPO_WRITE_BLOCKED`), not normal user deliverables under `user_files`/`task_drive`/`artifact_store`. Advanced evolves app code; Pro may leave protected edits on disk, but publication still requires the reviewed commit path. Runtime mode is a self-modification boundary, not an OS sandbox. Non-full safety modes emit durable `safety_mode_skip` audits.

All deterministic write/owner-control guards consume ONE mode-aware write-shape seam (`ouroboros/tools/write_shape.py`). Interpreter argv (incl. an `sh -c` wrap) takes `interpreter_write_shape`: a read-only `open(p, 'rb')` is not a write shape. Non-interpreter argv takes `non_interpreter_write_shape`: unconditional writers (cp/mv/rm/mkdir/touch/chmod…) keep the membership floor; pure-filter utilities (sort/uniq/sed/tar/gzip) are write-shaped only through a REAL channel (`sort -o` in both spellings; `sed -i` in any spelling plus in-script `w`/`W`/`e` and `-f` script files — a script not provably free fails closed; exotic non-`/` delimiters are the disclosed fail-open residual; a second uniq operand, tar create/extract, a redirect, a reported writer target). Bare prose words ('delete'/'trash'/'truncate') yield to the same read-carve the owner-control detectors use (`_is_pure_read_inspection`) — a provably read-only `grep -n delete ouroboros/safety.py` reads; an unprovable head fails closed. The protected-core mention branch consumes the same fact, so a pure read that merely mentions a protected filename is not a "modification". Write-mode opens (python `[wax+]`, perl `'>'`/`'>>'`), pathlib `.open('w')`, library save-APIs, ruby `File.delete`/`FileUtils.*`/`IO.binwrite`, opaque subprocess/exec escapes, shell indicators (redirects — token-initial or glued into an operand, `tee`, writer utilities) classify as writes; literal targets covered by `writer_target_tokens`; disclosed residuals (mode in a variable, an alias-unfollowable writer, parenless perl builtins) are covered for external workspaces by the runtime/secret read guard plus the LLM safety supervisor. Block messages name the resolved offending path and the sanctioned route (gated `read_file`/`write_file`, `root=skill_payload` with bucket/skill_name, or the selected process root) instead of one reasonless string.

Read-only shell git is allowed everywhere; mutating shell git only when its resolved target is outside the Ouroboros system repo and runtime data drives (`git_shell_policy`); network-disabled tasks still fence network git. Acting `self_worktree` children stay read-only because patch capture requires an unmoved HEAD. `git init`/`git clone` are judged by their destination, including relative destinations and path-valued retargeting flags. In external-workspace mode the runtime/secret read guard exempts only an all-read-only git command: mixed segments, `--no-index`, or a nominally read-only command with a writing `--output` path lose the exemption. `resolve_shell_cwd` canonicalizes the cwd once; every guard consumes that same path.

The generic Tool API VCS family (`vcs_status`, `vcs_diff`, `vcs_pull_ff`, `vcs_restore`, `vcs_revert`) defaults to `root=active_workspace` and accepts explicit `root=system_repo`; every result names the logical root and physical repo. Protected Ouroboros names constrain generic restore/revert only on the explicit system target — a project's own `BIBLE.md`/`contracts/` stays ordinary project content. `preflight_review`, `commit_reviewed`/`vcs_commit_reviewed`, `vcs_rollback`, and promotion remain system-repo lifecycles even in a project-focused task.

A task contract may declare `resource_policy.protected_artifacts[]` as execute-only black boxes: registry guards allow the declared execution but refuse reads, copies, hashes, static inspection, and trace/debug wrappers over those paths; generated outputs remain ordinary artifacts unless separately protected. Light-mode cognitive writes are redirected to `update_identity`/`update_scratchpad`/`knowledge_write` instead of raw memory-file edits; a corrected cognitive redirect is advisory, an ignored user-file root correction is a blocking deliverable failure.

### Review delivery (retired Claude runtime)

The Claude Agent SDK gateway is fully retired (owner-consented, 2026-08-29); its read-only api-route advisory moved onto the review substrate as the bounded native inspection episode (`review_native_episode.py`): an in-process episode of ≤ the configured round cap of `chat(tools=…)` calls against a fresh instance-local inspection registry (read_file/list_files/search_code/query_code/vcs_status/vcs_diff only, `local_readonly_subagent`, network/web off), every physical send ledger-accounted, caps fail closed typed. Mutating external coding work uses the delegated subagent path.

Review delivery has two closed route kinds in `review_execution.py`: `api_chat` and `agent_session`; vendor/harness names are route targets, not new kinds. A slot is bound to one immutable route before its first send and never falls back. The API executor lazily builds and memoizes the assembled review messages, so its durable prompt record and at-most-two physical sends use the same bytes. One logical interaction may use one bounded second send for transport or empty-output recovery, never while a dispatched outcome is unknown; task acceptance may also spend that second send on malformed-format repair. Transport, parsing, extraction, and semantic verdict remain distinct.

The hosted-agent executor starts one read-only delegated session through the shared Claudexor nanny; the session receives route-owned instructions and retrieval pointers and uses its own tools — it does not assemble the API review pack. A conforming structured result is preferred when the live harness manifest supports it; otherwise strict parsing runs first and a light extractor canonicalizes the transcript. Its typed verdict follows D19: `outputSchema` is asked only when the route's own live manifest declares structured output, trusted only on the run's own `outputConformance == "passed"`; extraction never launches a second hosted session. Custody, cancellation, full-artifact recovery, capability deltas, and settlement stay on the same delegated transport contract.

Advisory availability is evaluated from the current configured slot and route, never a stale stored verdict. A disabled advisory slot is an audited bypass; `api_chat` requires provider credentials for its RESOLVED model (same-model payable-spelling fallback included), while `agent_session` requires a resolvable session route. If the commit advisory is unavailable, the commit gate runs its compensating hermetic preflight only when tests remain independently applicable (caller did not explicitly skip them; diff not documentation-only); other bypasses record why tests were skipped. Optional skill advisory remains fail-open with disclosure. Malformed structured slot configuration is refused at save and becomes a typed loud review-time failure for commit triad, scope, advisory, plan, and skill review; task acceptance retains the explicit owner-approved residual — it uses the projected legacy/default API panel when that config is malformed. No surface silently chooses the opposite route.

### Usage ledger substrate vs. accounting policy

`usage_ledger.py` owns the durable append-only physical-attempt ledger: cross-process locking, sequence and transition validation, append+fsync, replay, and loud tail quarantine. `usage_accounting.py` is the one-way policy layer above it: route pricing, reservations, settlement, scopes, budget fences, imports, projections, admission. The substrate never imports policy — a structural boundary around the monetary authority: a pricing/budget-policy change cannot redefine valid ledger storage, and a locking/repair change cannot silently change what an attempt costs. Compatibility events, state mirrors, task fields, and UI projections may carry attempt ids and derived totals but never become a second charge source.
### Delegated subagents (Claudexor transport + the nanny)

**Coordination.** Children use `tree_note`/`tree_read`; only the parent may use `override_delegation_constraint`. A `review_requested` note carries an exact evidence reference/hash, wakes the waited/direct parent, and starts no reviewer or paid cycle; the parent/root may inspect it, hire a critic child, or let host-verified bytes enter the final root acceptance packet. Only the latter uses the acceptance wallet: immediately before reviewer transport the canonical root result atomically claims the candidate/evidence/fence binding under the effective cycle cap; a claim without its terminal host run is typed unknown, never re-dispatched. Children use descendant-scoped `forward_to_worker`, `peek_task`, `cancel_task`, `discard_child_result` for their own children (ancestry check grants no unrelated reach); workspace children keep scoped `knowledge_read`/`knowledge_list`; recursion never widens filesystem, budget, depth, deadline, commit, or owner authority.

**Actor SSOT.** `OUROBOROS_SUBAGENTS` is the active task-actor SSOT: strict `{enabled, items}`, at most ten `ConfiguredSubagent` rows. Each row: stable `subagent_id`, owner-authored English `recommended_use`, one normalized route (`api_model` or `agent_session`), optional effort, optional session credential pin; legacy `name` parses and is dropped. Description is selection context only — never parsed/ranked/mapped to task text.

**Scheduling.** `schedule_subagent` requires `subagent_id`, focused `objective`, `expected_output`; fields describe child context, constraints, memory, capabilities, write surface, narrower deadline, delegation budget, acceptance claims. No model-visible `model_lane`/`executor` axis, no public `effort` override — the selected row is the complete execution choice. Lineage, workspace/resource bounds, parent cognitive route, remaining budget are host-derived. Acceptance claims belong only to that child; blank values normalize away.

**Snapshot.** `subagent_runtime.select_subagent_snapshot` validates the exact id against the canonical enabled list and copies an immutable snapshot into the child (id, config fingerprint, description provenance, source, route, effort, selection time); Settings edits affect later children only. `api_model` → ordinary recursive API child on that exact model/effort, keeping the saved `provider::model` spelling (canonical ` (local)` marker removed for local calls; slash-normalized identity is telemetry, never transport). `agent_session` → recursive Ouroboros nanny bound by the same snapshot. Requested and effective engine facts stay separate in custody receipts; an explicit pin is strict, an empty pin delegates compatible-account rotation to Claudexor.

**Legacy lane.** The `model_lane`/`executor` resolver remains only for old durable records in one compatibility window. A legacy-only call is accepted only when its selectors map deterministically to exactly one migrated row; omitted/ambiguous `auto`, zero, or multiple matches return `subagent_selection_required`; supplying `subagent_id` with legacy selectors is a typed conflict. `schedule_subagent` reports the requested lane only — effective facts ride the dispatched child record. `subagents.resolve_subagent_dispatch` immediately hands a task carrying `configured_subagent` to `subagent_runtime`, so legacy Heavy/lane/executor policy cannot reinterpret an active selection.

**Recursion budget.** `delegation_budget` is the recursion authority: `may_delegate=false` refuses every descendant; `may_fan_out=false` permits one direct child and refuses later siblings; omitted legacy flags stay permissive; an incomplete task-result scan makes the child count unknown — only an explicit cap refuses on it. Depth provenance: `requested_depth`/`permitted_depth`/`attempted_depth`, host-visible `achieved_depth`; vendor-internal children do not advance achieved depth without a Claudexor boundary receipt. Persisted admission facts are monotonic, outranking later Settings changes, except global depth `0` (disables all new descendants) and the immutable hard ceiling. External ingress accepts only non-negative typed depths; malformed/negative rows terminalize before assignment. Over-cap writes a typed rejected child result; lower permitted depth is `capability_reduced`, not a flat tree.

**Waits.** `wait_task`/`get_task_result` return the full single-child handoff with a disclosed ten-row verification-receipt projection (outstanding red/masked pass first, newest remaining; exact omitted count). Readers union child-drive and canonical replicas with exact-row dedupe and stable chronology. `wait_tasks` stays batch-compact: `task_id, status, cost_usd, child_result_sha256, outcome_axes, result, trace_summary, capability_delta when the child has something to disclose, duplicate_of`. Both use `task_status.SETTLED_STATUSES`; pending cancellation is the typed `cancel_state: "pending"` projection (legacy `cancel_requested` read-path only), never completion. `wait_tasks` checks unknown ids, returns typed unknown rows plus a bounded direct-children roster, short-circuits an all-unminted set after the 30-second registration grace. `wait_task`/`wait_tasks`/`delegate_wait` may add an advisory cache-horizon note only when the latest send applied an explicit `5m`/`1h` TTL and the wait outlived it.

**Delegated-run cost.** Claudexor reports `summary.spendUsd` and `summary.spendEstimated`; `delegate_custody.disclosed_spend` is the single reader, returning `(amount, estimated)` together; `settle_run` and `_terminal_payload` both go through it. Runs request `authPreference: subscription` explicitly (the engine's `auto` default is subscription-first with invisible policy fallback to a paid key). Four cases: a DISCLOSED SETTLED zero settles `0.0` with `cost_final=true` (the free-session `subscription_session` ledger row, fed via `usage_accounting.record_subscription_session`; applied `credential_profile_id`/`access_profile` ride when disclosed); a disclosed settled charge rides the ledger as final money; an ESTIMATED amount rides with `cost_final=false` (estimated zero is not a proven free session); an UNDISCLOSED spend writes `cost_usd: null`, drops `cost_final`, increments `unknown_unmetered`. Tokens: `delegate_custody.disclosed_tokens` keeps `None` for an unreported count. DISCLOSED BOUND: undisclosed spend contributes `0.0` to `accounted_usd` (no fabricated number; the gap is represented); a `TOTAL_BUDGET` fence cannot stop undisclosed spend — it gets an honest loss of finality. Closing this needs a spend disclosure from Claudexor.

**Nanny model.** An `agent_session` subagent is a recursive task-tree child acting as a **nanny** supervising at most one active bounded external leaf. The task node keeps lineage, authority, deadline, budget, acceptance, cancellation, and descendants; the harness process remains a non-recursive tool leaf — session rows do not flatten the tree. The nanny is the host: verification receipts stay host-authored, harness output is a claim to check, never proof. An `api_model` row has no nanny/leaf split; the API child is the recursive actor itself.

**Transport.** `gateways/claudexor.py` is pure transport: reads the daemon descriptor `{host, port, tokenPath}` — `<config_dir>/daemon/control-api.json` under `CLAUDEXOR_CONFIG_DIR`, falling back to `~/.claudexor/v3/daemon/control-api.json` — negotiates `POST /v2/handshake` with `X-Claudexor-Protocol-Major: 3`, refuses an engine older than `config.CLAUDEXOR_MIN_VERSION`. **Token custody:** the bearer token grants the ENTIRE `/v2` surface; it is read/held/used only inside this module, never in a `ToolContext`, child env, or harness sandbox. The client runs with `trust_env=False`. Discovery is pure I/O; production starts obtain a handshaken owned gateway from `claudexor_daemon.ensure_owned_gateway`, keeping lifecycle above transport.

**Custody is durable.** Authority is durable `delegate_run_*` rows in the event log (canonical/budget root; the module dict is pure memoization). A lookup answers OWNED, FOREIGN, or UNKNOWN. Every INTENDED start mints a fresh `new_invocation_id` (per-intention UUID) riding the wire as the `Idempotency-Key`; the content hash is only the LOOKUP identity, never the wire key. Reuse only by explicit token: unknown outcome returns `pending_invocation_id`; `retry_of` replays the STORED canonical body under the SAME key (a re-derived body 409s). `reconcile_orphaned_runs` settles/cancels every open run whose owner is gone — the same owner-is-gone predicate `process_custody.reap_orphaned_processes` uses; its in-process twin `release_task_runs` runs at the loop's resource-release point. `maxSeconds` is damage limitation, never custody.

**Verified terminal only.** `delegate_cancel` returns four typed outcomes: `confirmed` (run reads back terminal), `requested` (accepted, not obeyed yet), `failed` (reachable daemon refused while the run keeps mutating), `containment_fault_run_may_still_be_live` (unverifiable). The last two record a durable containment fault surfacing as a CRITICAL health invariant until a terminal receipt or settlement clears it. `cancel_and_verify` short-circuits on the same `settled` fact `settle_run` uses; a refused control is never a verdict about the run — the state read decides.

**404 scoped to the daemon.** `daemon_says_absent` distinguishes a reachable daemon's explicit absence from transport ignorance. For a project registration, absence discharges the retirement obligation. For a run, it does not prove a child vanished across reprovisioning; custody closes the run as `delegate_run_closed_absent` (unreachable, not settled) only after registration retirement, without inventing terminal detail, usage, or spend. Shared registrations use the lowest run id as the deterministic retirement retry owner; an unreachable daemon still produces a containment fault.

**Settlement follows the durable fact.** Two independent idempotent obligations (ledger row + retiring a registration WE created); `settled` is claimed only when both landed AND the settlement row itself landed. `emit` returns `append_jsonl`'s success signal: a start whose row did not land reports `started_uncustodied` with `custody_durable: false`. `RunCustody` replays the settled row's `terminal_state`, disclosing `owner_task_id`/`run_settled`/`run_terminal_state`.

**Large result delivered, not severed.** `finalSummary`/`primaryOutput` carry the real work; Claudexor returns a preview up to 256 KiB while the generic cap head-truncates at 15k. `delegate_wait` bounds itself against `tool_capabilities.tool_result_limit`, stages the whole terminal detail at `task_drive/delegated_runs/<run>.json`, and returns a typed `output_delivery` block: `complete`, `consumed`, total chars, artifact reference with line count and sha256, and the `read_file(root='task_drive', …, start_line=N, max_lines=M)` recipe. Cut fields are RENAMED to `*_preview`; the result is NOT consumed until the artifact is read in full.

**Registration required.** First `delegate_start` against an unregistered root is answered HTTP 404 `project_not_registered`; the nanny registers the root first. `RunScope.ephemeral` exists but is not yet used, so a registration WE created is retired when the run settles; a pre-existing registration is left alone. Exception: a STABLE-target registration (`delegate_registration_policy.persistent_registration`) is marked `project_persistent` and survives EVERY retire path (settlement, orphan sweep, recovered-invocation refusals, pre-run refusal); its duty is discharged durably (`PROJECT_RETIRED` with `project_kept`), and any persistent sharer makes the shared project undeletable for siblings.

**Daemon lifecycle owned, explicit, lazy.** Reading status, booting, `delegate_wait`/`delegate_cancel` never install or spawn. Connect, `delegate_start`, reviewer-session start, and the executor probe call `claudexor_daemon.ensure_owned_gateway`. Explicitly opening Agents wakes only an already-provisioned `stale` home. The seam installs/repairs the exact reviewed target via `claudexor_runtime`, then starts the daemon under the isolated `CLAUDEXOR_CONFIG_DIR`; new package = archive in immutable resources, older package = pinned public URL. The pin carries exact official Node archives per host; packaged Node wins, else the review-bound platform archive into `data/state/cx/node` (schema-1 daemon-only / schema-2 CLI-capable metadata stay valid; malformed/future schemas are a typed failure). A CLI-capable pin promotes the npm subtree on POSIX (Windows daemon-only); the CLI resolver requires that managed POSIX pair. Every ensure runs the best-effort rotation reconcile: GET settings, conditional POST defaulting ONLY absent per-harness limit actions to `rotate` — persisted policy never overwritten, kind-aware engine skipped, real change leaves the durable `state/claudexor_rotation_provisioning.json` receipt.

**Live daemon not an update casualty.** A new pin is extracted and probed beside the serving daemon; the staged version becomes active only on the next natural start. No serving daemon + unprepared target = typed start failure; no fallback to an arbitrary PATH install. `OUROBOROS_CLAUDEXOR_BIN` is the one explicit operator override. The daemon stays session-scoped; stop is own-only-if-self-started.

**External-terminal login recovery** binds to the useful serving state. Before profile registration/setup-job creation, the handshake's exact engine version, build SHA, and absolute entry locate one preserved managed tree and its Node version; a fresh side-effect-free probe must repeat the handshake identity and advertise the additive `setup_attach` role — a probeless engine yields a typed 409 with no mutation, a failed/identity-mismatched probe yields retryable 503. The resolved argv is retained, receives the job id, and is rendered as inert POSIX or PowerShell text.

**Four nanny verbs** (`tools/delegate.py`): `delegate_start`, `delegate_wait`, `delegate_cancel`, `delegate_answer`. No fake `hurry`. `delegate_start` takes an exact `agent_session` `subagent_id` (or recovery-only `retry_of`); API actor ids are refused there.
