A project is a focused working room, not an isolated sub-mind: unified dialogue memory stays available, while an executing project task preferentially receives its own thread, journal, workpad, and project knowledge. `project_facts.py` routes facts to `projects/<id>/knowledge`; subagents inherit the root's resolved project id and never derive a new one; no per-project identity or scratchpad; only the current project's store injects into its focused task context.

The projects registry owns immutable project identity, canonical chat id, optional working directory, lifecycle/tombstone state, routing generation, activity revision. Admission persists the resolved project id in the task; `project_lease.py` permits one top-level writer per project while allowing that task's own subagent tree. Binding/history files support routing/presentation, not the lease. Delete closes routing, cancels/quiesces the tree, tombstones only after settlement; id, history, bindings, folder, journal, workpad, memory preserved.

`ensure_project_scope` can create or bind the current root to one project during execution: marks the live queue/lease surface under the queue lock before persisting, idempotent for the same project, refuses a second scope, cannot be invoked by a child to escape. Mid-task project creation is a structural capability, not a directory convention.

Project `journal.jsonl` records curated milestones; `workpad.md` retains active context. Focused context includes the workpad in full + recent journal rows with a visible pointer to older entries (never silent prefix slicing). On root completion, only high-signal swarm blockers/questions/interface contracts/contracts mirror once from the ephemeral task-tree ledger into the durable journal; ordinary chatter does not. A finished root whose effective tree is not the registered `working_dir` (or none registered) gets one typed "work lives at <path> @ <sha>" journal row from facts the task record already holds — no git subprocess — so an off-registry tree stays visible to later continuation promotions. A project digest gives consciousness a concise completion signal without pretending it is the raw project memory.

`promote_chat_to_task`, `route_to_project`, and `steer_task` succeed only after their token-matched supervisor facts are durable (task result, queue snapshot, annotation, or mailbox authority). With several candidates the LLM chooses; code auto-delivers only the unambiguous one-target case. An unconfirmed/stale receipt fails visibly and cannot launch a second root; these reuse the normal task lane — no parallel scheduler or message history. A routing/promote decision turn receives host-built ground truth, not chat memory: the Main routing manifest carries each project's registry `working_dir` + bounded typed projections of recent results (identity, outcome, workspace facts, artifact references — never raw result text); a project-room turn additionally gets that thread's most recent result in the same bounded form. The lookup reads the registry's durable `last_task_result_id` pointer first (stamped at project-task finalization), fetching one file regardless of newer foreign results; an absent/stale pointer falls back to the bounded newest-first mtime scan, then a disclosed full-store scan (lazy self-heal for pre-pointer projects; zero matches → nothing written back, so it repeats per lookup until a match). Only the absent-pointer case writes back: a non-empty pointer that failed to resolve is typically a split-drive result whose canonical copy-back has not landed, and overwriting would regress the pointer. On a Swarm router turn the host-owned room still chooses scope, but only on a genuine conflict; in a projectless room an explicit `project_name` is inherited — the project is created and bound before the root launches.

Canonical owner routing and project UI are projections over those same task/chat/binding/registry/result authorities. A routing receipt proves admission or mailbox delivery, not completion; an unread indicator proves a visible revision, not memory isolation. This keeps room organization, focused context, and durable facts useful without fragmenting identity or creating a second scheduler, ledger, or review system.
### Skills and extensions

Skill capability grows through distinct gates: discovery and manifest parsing (`skill_loader.py`), content-hash-bound review (`skill_review.py` / `skill_review_runner.py`), owner grants, dependency reconciliation (`marketplace/isolated_deps.py`), enablement, readiness (`skill_readiness.py`), and execution (`tools/skill_exec.py`). Discovery establishes identity, source, provenance, conflicts, and hash — not trust. Review status, grants, enablement, and dependency health remain independent durable facts under `data/state/skills/<name>/`. A visible/enabled skill is not executable until `skill_readiness_for_execution()` confirms the current payload satisfies every required gate.

A skill may declare a reviewed `presence:` behavior profile: instructions (inline or in a reviewed payload file), full knowledge topics, `main`/`light` runtime defaults with a bounded inline-round limit, and portable capability requests. Installation-local selections resolve those requests to exact built-in, extension, MCP, script, or confined resource targets. Admission requires the bound behavior skill installed, enabled, freshly reviewed, and complete for every required request, then compiles one immutable positive capability ceiling. The registry exposes and executes only targets inside that ceiling; the ceiling is copied through `task_contract`, never reconstructed from mutable skill or Settings state during a turn.

Bundled native skills and editable marketplace/user payloads occupy separate payload-plane buckets; owner and review state stays outside the payload. Declared conflicts are symmetric between enabled peers and never cause either payload to be deleted. `extension_loader.py` and the isolated-dependency layer load only a ready, hash-matching extension — a review PASS alone does not prove dependencies installed or widget/extension loadable.

`skill_lifecycle_queue.py` serializes install, update, review, grant, enable, and removal work, exposing queued/running/succeeded/failed plus stale metadata (recovery evidence, never a fake unlock of a still-running thread). Scheduled work is reconciled by `resync_skill_schedules()` and runs only after `skill_readiness_for_execution()`; schedule evaluation is DST-aware via the shared cron/timezone contract. Evolution stays hard-blocked in `light` runtime mode. These separations keep discovery, a UI toggle, or old review state from becoming execution authority.

#### Skill publication

The passive installed-skill projection never launches Betterleaks nor claims the current bytes are publication-ready; it exposes visibility and the independent `task_start_allowed` fact. Selecting Publish calls `POST /api/skills/{skill}/publish-preflight`: resolves one current payload, captures and scans its bytes, recomputes review staleness, returns exactly one backend-authored state (`ready`, `warnings`, `needs_attention`, `repairable`, `hard_block`); the browser renders only those. Warnings require explicit continuation; `needs_attention`/`repairable` still admit the ordinary managed `skill_publish` task; only `hard_block` prevents task creation.

Authoritative flow: `passive index (no scan) → selected preflight → explicit confirmation → ordinary managed task → fresh immutable capture bound to the stored review hash → payload scan → GitHub read-only planning → derived-output scans → first GitHub mutation → validated same-skill pull-request receipt → ordinary acceptance`. Every outbound byte derives from the capture; the live payload is neither reread nor rehashed to authorize the transaction. Literal Betterleaks `high` findings block the current outbound call; lower/unknown confidence stays a redacted warning. Packaged installs resolve the bundled `betterleaks-standalone`; source checkouts the exact managed runtime installed via `python -m ouroboros.betterleaks_runtime install`; Publish never downloads it. Recoverable failures return typed stage, completed-effect, and repair-hint facts under the DEVELOPMENT LLM-first rule. A top-level `skill_publish` task is accepted as successful only when pre-truncation metadata contains a validated pull-request receipt for the requested skill and configured Hub repository; the receipt never manufactures PASS, earlier failures stay visible, and a later valid same-skill receipt may satisfy the narrow prerequisite.

### MCP and browser-facing external tools

`mcp_client.py` owns configured HTTP/SSE and local stdio MCP discovery and invocation. HTTP/SSE entries validate URLs and auth headers. `secret_masking.py` owns the shared exact MCP token placeholder shapes used by status and Settings; load-time legacy repair stays intentionally limited to top-level Settings secrets (pre-existing nested MCP values are not migrated). Stdio entries pass one executable `command` and an exact string `args` list directly to the MCP SDK — no shell, custom environment, or custom working directory; the SDK context owns process shutdown. Settings shows URL/auth fields for HTTP/SSE and command plus one-argument-per-line args for stdio. When MCP is enabled, successfully discovered tools join the selected initial capability envelope. Discovery failure produces an explicit capability omission through `list_available_tools`, never a silently removed surface. Descriptions and results remain untrusted data; every call still crosses registry, resource, safety, timeout, and result-handling policy.
### Budget tracking

`usage_accounting.py` is the single monetary policy authority for core-mediated model work over the physical-attempt ledger; every physical provider send executes through `usage_accounting.execute_physical_attempt` with a unique attempt id and the durable lifecycle `reserved → dispatched → settled | unresolved`, or `reserved → released` before dispatch. A marked `dispatched` row may enter `released` only through the typed pre-dispatch transport seam (connection/pool failures that prove no request bytes could have been sent); ordinary timeouts and unknown errors remain `unresolved`. Each retry is a new attempt. For every inspectable application candidate, that attempt id binds exact post-transform raw/context identities and the existing-CAS physical manifest before dispatch; a persistence or host-bound Main shrink precondition can release the reservation without claiming a provider call. Specialized SDK/stream boundaries outside the candidate seam keep the lifecycle receipt but are labelled opaque rather than claiming an exact payload digest. The wrapper covers main/direct calls, children, scouts, all review surfaces, safety, synthesis, reflection, consciousness, transport/format retries, and opaque SDK calls; root scopes include their task tree and post-task/review work exactly once. Opaque adapters reserve their declared maximum and settle from provider cost when available. A reviewed external script or extension with model credentials is unknown/unmetered at each host-observed opaque execution boundary unless authoritative settlement exists; ordinary non-model skill work does not make the root non-final.

Before summary, reflection, or consolidation, the root freezes one shared ledger snapshot (settled subtree cost, live reservations, unresolved upper bound, unknown/unmetered count, integrity, timestamp, explicit non-final/partial state); all post-task consumers receive the same snapshot. The final terminal checkpoint remains the only final cost authority; a read failure is unavailable/null, never `$0`; there is no reconciliation LLM or parallel cost ledger.

The in-task pacing stop is resolved once by `task_pacing.resolve_cost_ceiling` as a typed `CostCeiling`: `disabled`/`active`/`exhausted_soft_land`/`unknown`. An active ceiling is the minimum of the configured percentage of global remaining budget and the root-tree cap minus one small absolute planning margin; either finite axis works without the other. The loop decides against subtree-accounted spend incl. in-flight holds, disclosing an own-cost fallback as a lower bound when tree accounting is unavailable. Graceful finalization runs before the ledger fence and never weakens it; unknown is not zero.

Pre-dispatch pricing is an exact-route, bounded, best-effort lookup from the provider's current catalog. Only the normalized exact model id and provider-supplied fields count; no manual price table, prefix inheritance, numeric fallback, or admission allowlist disguised as pricing. Unknown price is nullable and fail-open for admission while known spend remains below its limits; it reserves `None` and settles from provider-reported cost or a later exact price. If neither exists, cost stays `None` and `cost_final=false`. Unknown is not zero and does not excuse an exhausted known budget or a known reservation exceeding the remainder. A rejection settles at confirmed zero only when structural provider evidence proves it happened before upstream generation with zero usage; generic auth/quota/policy/timeout/transport failures keep their unresolved bound unless equivalent evidence exists.

`review_wave_admission` applies the same per-attempt math plus the root remainder before skill, plan, or task-acceptance reviewers launch, and the managed-update assisted-apply admission floor reuses the same estimator against the global budget remainder (`remaining_usd_override`) before any destructive merge step; it does not govern the P3 commit gate. An unpriced slot is disclosed and contributes no invented price while priced siblings still bind — one unknown route cannot disable admission control for the rest of a paid wave.

Validation, reservation, transition, append, and fsync share one short cross-process lock; network work stays outside it. A torn tail is quarantined loudly, the validated prefix remains readable, and affected projections stay integrity-degraded and non-final because paid work may be missing. Failed settlement persistence leaves the attempt dispatched/unresolved. A root budget refusal is durable and may be cleared on resume only after proving that no paid dispatch occurred or that a typed replay-safe checkpoint exists.

Interactive `usage_breakdown`/`usage_projection` reads use the validated-rows memo: `_read_new_records_locked` resumes from `LedgerResumeState`, validates device/inode, size, alignment, sequence, transition legality, and same-size rewrite signals, and folds only appended bytes; any distrust falls back to the normal full locked replay, which alone may quarantine. The monetary write paths (reserve/settle/transition/release/legacy-import) read through their own in-lock warm cache of the last validated full read (`_usage_rows_memo._read_records_locked_cached`): the same resume-fingerprint discipline parses only appended bytes under the held lock, any doubt falls back to the authoritative full locked read, and the cache keeps full ordered records because seq assignment and whole-history append validation need them (bounded to 8 drive roots, LRU). `_append_rows_locked` also guards its byte boundary: a crash can leave a newline-less final row, and the append prepends the missing newline so a torn tail costs at most itself. Both caches change read cost, never accounting meaning. The same memo also carries a fingerprint-keyed cache of finished `usage_projection`/`usage_breakdown` renders — cleared on refold and on every non-empty advance, never populated for a non-resumable crash-tail fingerprint, and served as deep copies.

For a root task, `GET /api/tasks/{id}` derives `cost_breakdown` at read time from the same ledger: own spend, child spend, unattributed spend, disclosed delegated spend, subscription sessions, `unknown_unmetered`/`non_final_rows`, finality, and authority. It is never persisted and is not a third sum. Non-root details omit it; an unreadable or unattributable ledger omits the entire object rather than returning a confident zero.

`state.json`, task results, `llm_usage`, `/api/state`, and `/api/cost-breakdown` are compatibility projections only; `state/usage_attempts.jsonl` remains the attempt authority. Startup's resumable importer records source hashes, archives non-secret legacy evidence, imports only attributable usage, and represents ambiguous or residual history explicitly without rewriting source logs or fabricating attempts.
## 7. Configuration (ouroboros/config.py)

Single source of truth for:
- **Paths**: HOME, APP_ROOT, REPO_DIR, DATA_DIR, SETTINGS_PATH, PID_FILE, PORT_FILE
- **Constants**: RESTART_EXIT_CODE (42), AGENT_SERVER_PORT (8765)
- **Settings defaults**: all model names, budget, timeouts, worker count
- **Functions**: `load_settings()`, `save_settings()`, `apply_settings_to_env()` (copies hot-reloadable/runtime keys — models, API keys, GitHub integration settings, update channel, review/effort settings, local-model config, and `OUROBOROS_RUNTIME_MODE` + `OUROBOROS_SKILLS_REPO_PATH` — into `os.environ`), `normalize_runtime_mode()` (SSOT clamp shared by `server.py::api_settings_post`, `_coerce_setting_value`, `ouroboros/onboarding_wizard.py::prepare_onboarding_settings`), `get_runtime_mode()` / `get_skills_repo_path()` (read-side helpers for `gateway/state.py::api_state`); `ouroboros/update_channels.py` owns `get_update_channel()` / `get_update_branch()`, `acquire_pid_lock()`, `release_pid_lock()`

Settings file: `~/Ouroboros/data/settings.json`, file-locked. `secret_masking.py` is the wire-placeholder authority for known and owner-defined top-level secrets; `load_settings()` repairs only recognized disk placeholders before env precedence, so a real env credential is never masked; `prepare_settings_for_persist()` applies the same repair at the writer boundary; password/token/MCP masks stay context-specific.

`ouroboros/openrouter_attribution.py` is the application-identity SSOT for every first-party paid OpenRouter request: its canonical public URL is the primary application id and `X-OpenRouter-Title` the display name; a fork must use its own URL.

### LLM output token budgets

Ouroboros uses provider-specific names for the same output-token budget: OpenRouter/Anthropic-compatible calls send `max_tokens`; official direct OpenAI Chat routes send `max_completion_tokens` through `LLMClient._build_remote_kwargs`. Direct OpenAI sends requested `reasoning_effort` provider-wide; model-name prefixes are not capability authority, and only exact-route success-confirmed wire evidence may adapt it. Runtime floors:

| Surface | Output-token budget |
|---------|---------------------|
| `LLMClient.chat()` / `chat_async()` defaults | 65,536 |
| Main task loop (`loop_llm_call.MAIN_LOOP_MAX_TOKENS`) | 65,536 |
| `LLMClient.vision_query()` and VLM tools (`analyze_screenshot`, `vlm_query`) | 32,768 |
| Review synthesis dedup | 16,384 |
| Chat block consolidation, era compression, scratchpad consolidation | 16,384 |
| Execution reflection and pattern-register update | 16,384 |
| Improvement-backlog grooming (`improvement_backlog.groom_backlog`) | 8,192 |
| Post-task evolution promotion decision (`post_task_evolution`) | 8,192 |
| Task summary and chat/history summary tool | 16,384 |
| Context compaction round summaries | 32,768 |
| Skill publish PR body generation | 8,192 |
| Background consciousness loop | 65,536 |
| Project naming LIGHT one-shot (`project_naming.llm_project_name`) | 256 |
| Provider Test (`llm_probe.PROVIDER_TEST_MAX_TOKENS`) | 16 |

| Key | Default | Description |
|-----|---------|-------------|
| OPENROUTER_API_KEY | "" | Default multi-model router key |
| OPENAI_API_KEY | "" | Official OpenAI provider key (runtime + web search) |
| OPENAI_BASE_URL | "" | Custom/legacy OpenAI-compatible runtime base URL; empty keeps official OpenAI `web_search` |
| OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_API_KEY | "" | Dedicated OpenAI-compatible provider key |
| OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_BASE_URL | "" | Dedicated OpenAI-compatible provider base URL |

| GIGACHAT_CREDENTIALS | "" | GigaChat authorization key (base64 `client_id:secret`, OAuth); enables `gigachat::...` via the `gigachat` library |
| GIGACHAT_USER | "" | GigaChat basic-auth username (alternative to `GIGACHAT_CREDENTIALS`) |
| GIGACHAT_PASSWORD | "" | GigaChat basic-auth password (with `GIGACHAT_USER`) |
| GIGACHAT_SCOPE | `GIGACHAT_API_PERS` | Scope: `GIGACHAT_API_PERS` personal / `GIGACHAT_API_B2B` prepaid / `GIGACHAT_API_CORP` pay-as-you-go |

| GIGACHAT_VERIFY_SSL_CERTS | `true` | Verify GigaChat TLS; `false` to skip (e.g. Russian Trusted Root CA) |
| GIGACHAT_PROFANITY_CHECK | "" | Optional `true`/`false` profanity filter, read by the library |
| ANTHROPIC_API_KEY | "" | Direct Anthropic routing (`anthropic::...`), incl. advisory same-model payable-spelling fallback for direct-key installs |
| MINIMAX_API_KEY | "" | MiniMax direct provider key (`minimax::...`, OpenAI-compatible) |
| MINIMAX_REGION | "" | `global_en` (api.minimax.io, default) or `cn_zh` (api.minimaxi.com); selects base URL per call; applies next task |
| transport-skill requested bot token | "" | Stored secret for the Telegram bridge skill after owner grant |
| transport-skill local chat id | "" | Stored setting for the Telegram bridge skill |
| OUROBOROS_NETWORK_PASSWORD | "" | Non-loopback auth gate; empty keeps open bind but logs a warning |
| OUROBOROS_SERVER_HOST | 127.0.0.1 | Server bind host; `0.0.0.0` for LAN/Docker; restart required |
| OUROBOROS_UPDATE_CHANNEL | `stable` | Feed: `stable`→`main`, `qa`→`ouroboros-stable`, `development`→`ouroboros`; applies immediately |
| OUROBOROS_MANAGED_UPDATE_FETCH_TIMEOUT_SEC | 300 | Ceiling for non-interactive official fetch/tag discovery; timed-out trees killed |
| OUROBOROS_RESCUE_GIT_TIMEOUT_SEC | 300 | Per-process ceiling for the rescue graph; timed-out trees killed, disclosed nonzero; managed rollback stays fail-open |
| OUROBOROS_TRUST_NONLOCAL_BIND_WITHOUT_PASSWORD | unset | Env-only Docker/K8s escape hatch: `1` lets Settings save with wildcard bind and no password; only behind ingress auth/VPN |
| OUROBOROS_MODEL | google/gemini-3.7-flash | Main reasoning model (primary real default; worker slots below are empty→Main unless noted) |
| OUROBOROS_MODEL_HEAVY | "" | **Legacy migration/history only.** May seed an API Available-subagent row when `OUROBOROS_SUBAGENTS` is absent; excluded from active keys/Provider Test/startup/scheduling |
| OUROBOROS_MODEL_LIGHT | openai/gpt-5.6-luna | Fast/cheap model for safety/compact routing/helpers; may seed the Fast scout. Empty uses Main. Never forces a session nanny's route |
| OUROBOROS_MODEL_VISION | "" | Vision/caption slot. Empty uses Main for remote routes; local/blind routes need an explicit reachable slot |
| OUROBOROS_IMAGE_INPUT_MODE | auto | `auto` inline for vision-capable / captions for blind; `caption` always; `inline` when supported; `off` placeholders |
| OUROBOROS_VISION_CAPTION_TIMEOUT_SEC | 90 | Dead-socket ceiling for send-time captions/VLM helpers; direct caption unwrapped, helpers one settlement margin; tool timeout may widen |
| OUROBOROS_MODEL_CONSCIOUSNESS | "" | BG Consciousness slot; empty uses Main; never silently downgrade this lane |
| OUROBOROS_MODEL_FALLBACKS | openai/gpt-5.6-luna | Cross-model fallback chain on no usable response (429-aware cooldown, deduped, active dropped; all-one-model dedupes to no-op) |
| OUROBOROS_MODEL_MAX_CONCURRENCY | 3 | Max concurrent calls per (model, use_local) route; excess threads wait, deadline-bounded (`model_concurrency.py`); <=0 disables |
| OUROBOROS_MODEL_SLOT_MAX_WAIT_SEC | 180 | Ceiling a call waits for a concurrency slot with no deadline; past it proceeds unslotted |
| OUROBOROS_PROJECT_NAMING_TIMEOUT_SEC | 60 | Transport timeout for LIGHT project-naming (`project_naming.py`) |
| OUROBOROS_PROJECT_NAMING_ASYNC_TIMEOUT_SEC | 8 | Gateway HARD wait for inline project name before heuristic fallback (`llm_project_name_async`) |
| OUROBOROS_FALLBACK_COOLDOWN_ENABLED | true | Fail-soft: park a just-failed model (429/5xx/overload) briefly so fallback/swarm skip it |
| OUROBOROS_FALLBACK_COOLDOWN_SEC | 120 | Cooldown window for a transiently-failed model |
| OUROBOROS_FALLBACK_ATTEMPTS_PER_MODEL | 1 | Per-fallback transient-retry cap (1–2) |
| OUROBOROS_REVIEW_NATIVE_MAX_ROUNDS | 16 | Round cap for the native review inspection episode (one `chat(tools=…)` per round); exhaustion = typed fail-closed refusal |
| OUROBOROS_REVIEW_NATIVE_MAX_TRANSCRIPT_CHARS | 900000 | Transcript cap for the native review episode; exceeding = typed fail-closed refusal |
| OUROBOROS_MODEL_DEEP_SELF_REVIEW | openai/gpt-5.6-sol-pro | Dedicated deep self-review slot |
| OUROBOROS_MAX_WORKERS | 10 | Worker pool size |
| OUROBOROS_MAX_ACTIVE_SUBAGENTS_PER_ROOT | 6 | Active subagent cap per root (~500 = `MAX_ACTIVE_SUBAGENTS_HARD_CAP`; near it `wait_tasks` may hit the 15K truncation — chunked waits + `get_task_result`) |
| OUROBOROS_MAX_SUBAGENT_DEPTH | 3 | Depth cap (max 10, min 0; **0 disables delegation** — roots still run; plan review in-process) |
| OUROBOROS_DISABLE_MANAGED_UPDATES | (unset) | Operator/stand lever, env only. `1` makes `git_ops.safe_restart` skip checkout but sync deps + import test; explicit Update/Rollback still apply |
| OUROBOROS_ALLOW_MUTATIVE_SUBAGENTS | (empty) | Acting-child gate. Explicit true/false = every surface; empty = Settings **Auto** (advanced/pro all; light external/genesis only, self-worktree off) |
| OUROBOROS_SUBAGENT_WORKTREE_ROOT | (empty) | Root for acting self_worktree checkouts; empty = ~/Ouroboros/subagent_worktrees |
| OUROBOROS_SUBAGENT_PROJECTS_ROOT | (empty) | Root for genesis subagent projects; empty = ~/Ouroboros/projects; never age-pruned |
| OUROBOROS_SUBAGENTS | (empty) | Available-subagents SSOT: `{enabled:boolean,items:[...]}`, ≤10 rows, one `api_model`/`agent_session` actor per stable `subagent_id`, `recommended_use`, route, optional effort/pin. Empty = bounded legacy/undecided resolution |
| OUROBOROS_SUBAGENT_HARNESS | (empty) | **Legacy migration/live-compat only**: parseable route seeds one migrated session row while canonical absent; `off` → disabled. Not active SSOT |
| OUROBOROS_SUBAGENT_PROFILE | (empty) | **Legacy sibling pin only**; preserved on the migrated row; new pins live in `route.credential_profile_id` |
| OUROBOROS_DELEGATE_WAIT_SEC | 120 | Internal transport window for quiet `delegate_supervision` sleep; not a model-visible `delegate_wait`; expiry does not wake the LLM |
| OUROBOROS_DELEGATE_WAIT_MAX_SEC | 1800 | Legacy ceiling for low-level wait windows; public supervision = meaningful events/checkpoint/outer deadlines |
| OUROBOROS_DELIVERABLES_ROOT | (empty) | Container for UNNAMED user deliverables; empty = ~/Ouroboros/Deliverables (never GC-pruned). Bare `user_files` filename lands here; explicit path honored under home. `user_files_path_block_reason` admits it past the overlap guard only as genuine sibling of hard drives |
| OUROBOROS_GC_RETENTION_DAYS | 7 | Startup GC age for ALL disposable artifacts (worktrees, task drives, service logs; max 365; SSOT `retention.py`) |
| OUROBOROS_RESTART_DRAIN_MAX_SEC | 120 | Agent restarts drain work, then persist exact handoffs only for custody-durable nannies before exit 42; owner restart = no-resume |

**Direct-provider review fallback** (legacy name: OpenAI-only review fallback). When exactly one official direct provider is configured, `config.get_review_models()` compiles that provider's declarative reviewer-role sequence using provider-prefixed model IDs. Covers official OpenAI, Anthropic, MiniMax, Cloud.ru, and GigaChat; OpenRouter, legacy-base, OpenAI-compatible, and mixed-provider configs stay outside. OpenAI and Anthropic run three independent Main-model slots; MiniMax its mixed Main/Light panel; Cloud.ru and GigaChat use their one role model for every slot. `_exclusive_direct_remote_provider_env` returns empty when OpenRouter, legacy `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, OpenAI-compatible keys, or multiple official direct providers are present; `provider_models.migrate_model_value` must make the main model already start with the exclusive provider prefix, preventing cross-provider free-text models from silently entering the direct-provider path. This self-sufficiency is the single-provider independence invariant (docs/DEVELOPMENT.md "Provider Independence").

**GigaChat specifics (`gigachat::`).** Routed via the native `gigachat` library (NOT OpenAI-compatible) in `llm.py::_chat_gigachat`. OpenAI `tools` → GigaChat `functions`; at most ONE `function_call` per turn → parallel `tool_calls` collapse to first. Role `tool` results become role `function`, valid JSON (plain text wrapped `{"result": ...}`); `system` first, later system-reminders demoted to `user`. `reasoning_effort` omitted — GigaChat-3 may burn the whole `max_tokens` budget on hidden reasoning and return empty content/tool_calls. Fresh direct-only installs use `GigaChat-2-Max` for every ordinary/review slot: `GigaChat-3-Ultra` limited to personal Freemium, Max across personal/legal-entity tariff scopes. No live cost source — cost nullable/unknown, never hand-maintained tariff. GigaChat below the 1M scope-review floor: GigaChat-only setup fills the slot like Cloud.ru. No ≥1M reviewer → owner-selected `low` (whole-repository scope review declaredly not performed, every commit records typed `skipped_low_context_mode`) or retrieving scope slot ≥200K sourced evidence; blocking triad still reviews the full staged diff in both modes (managed-update resolution commits review the declared M0→S subject).
## 8. Git Branching, CI, and Build

`ouroboros` is the local working branch; the runtime setting selects one official feed: Stable = newest plain release tag reachable from both `main` and `ouroboros-stable`, QA = the `ouroboros-stable` tip, Development = the `ouroboros` tip. Promotion and rollback are owner-controlled exact-SHA movements. Ordinary restart preserves the local tip; explicit update owns fetch, target validation, rescue, apply, rollback. `managed` is the official read/update remote, `origin` optional personal persistence. Desktop and Colab reject a target without a regular non-empty `BIBLE.md` before checkout. External PRs target `ouroboros`, allocate no release version, and receive the collision-free version only when maintainers land and re-review them on the current target.

The local `ouroboros-stable` ref is a recovery fallback maintained by explicit promotion; it does not select the official QA feed. Colab seeds it from the already validated shared Stable release when possible, else the selected validated channel, pinned until promotion. Launcher metadata describes bootstrap provenance only. Runtime status, preflight, Colab bootstrap, and apply resolve the selected channel and exact fetched SHA themselves, so an older frozen launcher cannot silently redirect updates. Stable additionally requires the shared plain release tag; QA and Development do not use version comparison as an admission gate.

### CI

The main CI workflow has five roles: fork-safe quick checks with no provider secrets; the full cross-platform matrix; trusted provider integration; official-skill install, preflight, review, dependency, and keyless execution smoke; tag-triggered build/release. Quick and full jobs each run a blocking `size_ratchet` pytest step — the ONLY enforcing surface for the repo size gates (local runs exclude the marker and warn): manifest exactness on the tip plus pairwise shrink-only transition against the event base in `OURO_SIZE_RATCHET_BASE_REF` (PR base SHA / push `event.before`; an all-zeros, missing, or unresolvable base degrades to the tip's parent manifest, verified against the parent's own tree — never a skip; a resolvable base without the manifest fails closed; only a checkout with no parent manifest at all bootstraps with the transition skipped). Checkouts fetch full history so the explicit base ref stays resolvable. Quick and full also run the browser-module suite (`cd web && node --test tests/*.test.js`) — the same node lane the hermetic commit gate executes through `ouroboros/preflight_node.py`, so client-side test parity holds both ways. Secret-bearing skill review runs before any step importing downloaded plugin code; a missing required key is red, not skipped. Release jobs build three platform archives, an AppImage, and three native Linux package assets, verify each final asset, produce checksums, SBOMs and source-bound attestations, and recheck the remote annotated tag against the event SHA before draft creation and publication.

Tool-schema compatibility has two CI layers. Fork-safe PR tests build the complete shipped built-in catalog (MCP/extensions not loaded), validate every schema as general JSON Schema plus the known cross-provider subset (no empty enum; no root `anyOf`/`oneOf`/`allOf`), and require the OpenRouter/function, direct Anthropic, GigaChat, and direct OpenAI projections to preserve the complete tool-name set. The trusted `integration` lane runs only on `main`/`ouroboros`/`ouroboros-stable` pushes, manual runs, and tags: one bounded `delegate_start` canary per physical route (returned call never executed) over that registry — OpenRouter Gemini (`google/gemini-3.7-flash`), Opus (`anthropic/claude-opus-5`), GPT (`openai/gpt-5.6-luna`), Grok (`x-ai/grok-4.6`), DeepSeek (`deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro-0813`); the three shipped direct OpenAI defaults; direct Anthropic (`anthropic::claude-sonnet-5`); optional MiniMax (`minimax::MiniMax-M3`), Cloud.ru (`cloudru::zai-org/GLM-4.7`), GigaChat (`gigachat::GigaChat-2-Max`). The shipped direct-OpenAI Main route alone keeps its second-turn nonce-bearing continuation. Each canary requires positive usage, exact provider/model identity, and a normalized schema-valid tool call; supported reasoning routes request `medium`; GigaChat uses its automatic tool choice. Missing core credentials are red only in the official trusted job; absent optional credentials are loud skips. Quota/billing, 429, 5xx, and timeout outcomes remain typed inconclusive; contract/auth/model/tool/reasoning 4xx are red. A canary turn gets one same-route second physical send only for a runtime-classified semantic-empty response, bypassing response caches where supported; a repeated empty response, permanent body/context error, or non-empty malformed/schema response stays red. The outer choice termination marker is retained in host-owned usage as `response_finish_reason` for bounded diagnostics only. A valid native call may carry assistant text — a bounded warning, not a failure; malformed raw arguments are reported by type/position and argument hash, never copying the provider payload.

Quick PR jobs are read-only, receive no provider secrets, and never use `pull_request_target`; there is no scheduled paid run. `release-preflight` depends on the trusted integration job and the full test matrix, so a reproducible provider-contract failure blocks tag builds while an inconclusive provider outage does not. The live catalog skill lane remains an independent release dependency: it proves published payloads still install on this runtime while keeping provider credentials out of every process that imports payload code. These external-service dependencies are explicit owner trade-offs, not accidental flakiness.

The separate Scorecard workflow runs on `main` pushes and weekly: every action pinned by full commit SHA, permissions defaulting to read-only, adding only `security-events: write` and `id-token: write` for SARIF upload and OpenSSF publication. `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` owns community rules and reporting; `CITATION.cff` the software citation and preferred technical-report citation; `site/paper/index.html` the canonical paper landing page; `docs/benchmarks/evidence.json` a historical, release-bound non-GAIA projection of public benchmark claims and immutable evidence links. README remains the claim SSOT.

The dependency snapshot workflow reads the managed Claudexor version from `ouroboros/claudexor_runtime_pin.json` and submits it to GitHub's dependency graph. It runs on `main` and `ouroboros` only when the pin or workflow changes, plus manual dispatch, needing only `contents: write`. The repository dependency-graph setting must be enabled before submission; a manual dispatch backfills the current pin after that owner setting changes. The snapshot describes the exact bundled runtime relationship without presenting Claudexor as a Python or Node package dependency.

### Build scripts

`build.sh`, `build_linux.sh`, `scripts/build_appimage.sh`, `scripts/build_linux_packages.sh`, `scripts/smoke_linux_packages.sh`, `build_windows.ps1`, and `scripts/build_repo_bundle.py` are release-invariant owners. Linux PyInstaller runs under the same pinned portable Python shipped in the payload, so its bundled libpython keeps the payload's glibc floor instead of inheriting the release runner's newer ABI. The AppImage builder wraps that payload with digest-pinned tool and runtime bytes; the native Linux builder wraps the same x86_64 payload without replacing its runtime. Native package metadata declares the external Git required by bootstrap; bundled Python, Node, and browser remain under `/opt/ouroboros`. The native packages install the opt-in user unit at `/usr/lib/systemd/user/ouroboros.service`; they contain no activation scriptlet. The builder prefers hardlinks in an output-local stage, falling back to one payload copy when hardlinking is unavailable. The release-gating smoke installs through `apt` or `dnf`, proving Git resolution, the desktop files, the installed user unit and its launcher/cgroup/no-restart contract, the real packaged CLI, and a bounded desktop-launcher start on Ubuntu 22.04/Fedora 42; Astra Linux and RED OS vendor-image runs remain explicit informational evidence because third-party registry availability cannot block publication. The macOS image keeps the explicit app, Applications symlink, and optional CLI installer layout; final-image verification checks the real symlink target. Release tag prerequisite: `scripts/build_repo_bundle.py` is the release-tag SSOT and verifies the annotated `v$(cat VERSION)` tag points at `HEAD` before packaging.

Betterleaks 1.8.1 follows the same release-resource discipline: packaged builds stage the pinned binary and license under `betterleaks-standalone`; source checkouts install the exact managed runtime explicitly under `data/state/betterleaks/`. The Publish path never downloads it; the real three-OS contract runs in branch CI, while final embedded-artifact receipts belong to the later tagged release.

Python dependency resolution has one authority: direct requirements and their runtime/desktop/browser/build group membership live in `pyproject.toml`, while `uv.lock` records the universal cross-platform solution under the pinned `tool.uv.required-version`. Source and CI sync the lock with `--locked`; metadata drift is an error, never a silently stale install. Packaging keeps its deliberate two-interpreter boundary through projections: build scripts export their temporary PyInstaller/desktop input directly from `uv.lock`; the committed `requirements-runtime.lock` supplies embedded `python-standalone` and managed updates that intentionally use pip without bundling uv; a one-line `requirements.txt` pointer lets already released N-1 updaters find the same export. CI regenerates the compatibility export and requires a clean diff, so neither file becomes an independent dependency authority.

Platform builds precompile bundled Python with unchecked-hash bytecode. Sealing valid bytecode prevents runtime `__pycache__` writes from invalidating a macOS signature; runtime children route caches outside the bundle. With signing enabled, hardened runtime, notarization, xattr hygiene, and strict verification remain part of the stable-release path. Prerelease artifacts may intentionally be unsigned, and their evidence must report the actual signing state rather than imply notarization. Linux and Windows use the same precompile for startup parity without a macOS seal.

The release proof begins with the final DMG, AppImage, tarball, or ZIP rather than its staging directory. Each platform shard checks the embedded repository bundle, packaged CLI, and managed Claudexor seed plus Node by starting the owned daemon, completing a fixture task, and verifying an identity-bound stop. The Linux AppImage is extracted for metadata and SBOM inspection, then run through the FUSE-independent runtime to prove exact version output, CLI dispatch, browser-fallback readiness, payload lifetime after CLI-started relaunch, main-executable libraries, and clean shutdown; the payload remains read-only at runtime. The nested cleanup proof follows the live `runtime → AppRun custodian → launcher` chain: stop the launcher recorded by the PID file, wait for the owning runtime, require both extraction and private base absent — browser-fallback evidence, not a claim of a native GTK/Qt backend. The macOS smoke also verifies its Finder installation surfaces. On Linux, the proven tarball payload is wrapped into the three native packages; each receives its own digest-bound package-manager smoke receipt after the installed user unit + key lifecycle directives, the packaged CLI, and the bounded desktop-launcher start all pass in the package-manager container, plus provenance attestation. A digest-pinned Syft build produces CycloneDX inventories from extracted payload bytes; the tarball inventory is reused for the three native wrappers instead of rescanning identical bytes. GitHub SBOM attestations bind the relevant payload inventory to each final asset. The release job accepts only the seven expected release assets, recalculates their digests, verifies both predicate types, writes the checksum/evidence capsule, and rechecks the remote tag immediately before publication. Signing credentials remain step-scoped and are absent from SBOM and attestation steps.

Public installer naming and links are part of that release projection. `release_sync.py::RELEASE_ASSET_TEMPLATES` is the filename SSOT shared by the proof builder, README, and the source/generated install pages. A version bump rewrites only named download references and `data-release-download` anchors to immutable `/releases/download/v{VERSION}/...` URLs; the versioned `/releases/latest/download/...` shape is forbidden because GitHub excludes prereleases from `latest`. Generated release notes expose direct links only for the seven assets already accepted by proof assembly.

The default repository README and legacy GitHub Pages deployment both use the stable `main` boundary (`main:/docs` for Pages), not the development `ouroboros` branch. Stable promotion advances `main` only after the release is published with all seven proof-bound installers. An unreleased development VERSION can therefore exist without exposing dead installer links to users; an omitted promotion leaves the previous working release public.

### Docker

Docker runs the web and server runtime without PyWebView. Non-loopback binding requires the explicit network-authentication policy; container packaging does not create a weaker owner boundary.
## 9. Shutdown & Process Cleanup

Closing the window or quitting must leave zero orphaned work. Normal shutdown signals the lifecycle loop, lets the server lifespan stop workers and services, waits for the recorded server process group or Job Object, escalates only when it remains alive, performs launcher-owned orphan cleanup, and releases the PID lock. Ordinary server teardown closes its own Host Service listener; blind port sweeps are reserved for launcher cleanup, recovery, and panic. Those listener sweeps encode reserved-port ownership, not process identity: an arbitrary direct or development listener on the configured runtime port or Host Service port may be terminated even though the identity reaper itself spared it. A success signal is emitted only after recorded death is verified.

Panic is a complete owner stop, not a restart. It stops consciousness, records the durable evolution owner-stop state, closes the campaign and queued promotion request, writes `panic_stop.flag`, stops the local model and any daemon this process itself spawned, then kills tracked foreground commands, executor processes, services, workers, and their process trees before the hard server exit. A daemon merely attached to this process is deliberately not killed; custody reconciles that disclosed residual on the next manual start. The launcher performs its final sweep and closes the window. On the next launch the panic or no-resume flag suppresses automatic work until the owner acts.

`run_command`, `run_script`, `start_service`, executor-backed processes, extension companions, delegated runs, and other long-lived children enter process custody with exact process identity. Unix process groups and Windows Job Objects provide tree cleanup; durable executor and service records let the host recover after worker death. Normal cleanup may archive logs, while panic skips nonessential finalization. Timeout and signal exits remain distinct in tool results so a killed command never resembles success.

## 10. Key Invariants

1. **Constitution and identity persist.** `BIBLE.md` is never deleted; `identity.md` remains a physical file even when its content evolves.
2. **Release metadata has one projection.** `VERSION` is canonical; `ouroboros/tools/release_sync.py::version_carrier_desyncs()` and `sync_release_metadata()` keep the PEP 440 form in `pyproject.toml` and the editable root entry in `uv.lock`, plus the author-facing version in `web/package.json`, `web/modules/api_types.js::GATEWAY_CONTRACT_VERSION`, the README badge, and this document's header. Changelog prose remains deliberate. Pull requests into `ouroboros` leave these carriers byte-identical to their target; integration assigns the release version.
3. **Configuration and messaging have single owners.** Defaults and paths live in `ouroboros/config.py`; messages go through `supervisor/message_bus.py`; concurrent state transitions use the owning file lock.
4. **The attempt ledger is monetary authority.** `state/usage_attempts.jsonl` records every physical model send. State, task, event, and UI totals are projections carrying attempt identity; unknown or unresolved cost never becomes false zero.
5. **Packaged bootstrap is manifest-bound.** A packaged install verifies `repo.bundle` and its manifest once, then runs the managed checkout. Restart preserves its local tip; only explicit update applies an approved exact SHA.
6. **Shutdown is custody-complete.** Normal close verifies child death, and panic stops all owned workers, process trees, services, cognition, and evolution without allowing agent code to delay it. Any intentionally attached-daemon residual is disclosed.
7. **Architecture stays current.** Structural owners, APIs, durable data, UI surfaces, and the rationale for non-obvious guards are updated here with the code; release chronology stays in git and README.
8. **Skill gates do not collapse.** Discovery, deterministic preflight, content-hash-bound executable review, owner grants, dependency readiness, enablement, and execution remain separate. A PASS does not install dependencies, and `enabled=true` does not prove executable readiness.
9. **Startup rescue has one mutation owner.** Supervisor recovery writes rescue evidence before reset or blocks while preserving the tree. Worker or agent construction remains warning-only and never stages or commits inherited dirt.
10. **Projection over replay.** Interactive status, history, and cost reads are bounded, non-materializing projections; durable owners perform the one authoritative replay or terminal materialization.
11. **UI resources carry a disposer.** Every subscription, listener, observer, timer, stream, and live page instance has explicit teardown; navigation does not leave hidden instances mutating visible or durable state.
12. **Frozen contracts extend explicitly.** `ouroboros/contracts/` remains versioned, backward-compatible ABI. New capability extends the frozen shape or uses an explicitly versioned successor; convenience code does not smuggle policy into it.
13. **Provider wire adaptation stays exact-route and success-confirmed.** Canonical history remains provider-neutral; typed physical projections may change values, fields, or a registered dialect on one provider/endpoint/API/model only. Failed candidates teach nothing durable, task-local cognition degradation never becomes future dispatch authority, and the physical-attempt ledger remains distinct from terminal request-wire history.

### 10.1 Continuity data-flow map

The table is the canonical map for continuity changes. A bounded view is an interface projection, never a new authority; the actor making the decision must be able to resolve the named source through an existing reader, otherwise the view is partial and the consumer remains non-final or abstains.

| Surface | Canonical source and owner | Bounded projection | Actor-readable source/ref | Decision and retention rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Owner authority and biography | Canonical `logs/chat.jsonl`, archive generations, `memory/dialogue_blocks.json` owned by the canonical drive | Main/Project context sections and archive-aware history windows | Existing `chat_history`/archive readers with generation and gap metadata | A known gap is disclosed; summaries/blocks never replace exact current owner directives. Raw generations and durable blocks follow their existing retention owner. |
| Execution evidence | Task results, observability call manifests/blobs, service logs, process-custody records | Status cards, terminal rows, bounded tails, compact child summaries | Exact artifact/blob/service-log refs carried by the task result or canonical promotion | A projection cannot certify a missing child/source. Referenced canonical artifacts are promoted before child-drive GC; disposable execution scratch follows unified GC. |
| Terminal task/project memory | Root terminal result plus existing task/project summary producers | Cognitive Main terminal summaries and the two Project-root UI lifecycle rows (started + terminal completion) | Task-result ID, project binding, summary/source refs | Summary is a biography projection, not raw evidence. Terminal outcomes, including failed/cancelled/degraded, remain retained through their canonical result owner. |
| Background Consciousness observations | `data/state/consciousness_observations.jsonl`, append-only enqueue/ACK rows owned by `BackgroundConsciousness` | Pending count/oldest metadata and a bounded recent observation rendering | `read_file(root='runtime_data', path='state/consciousness_observations.jsonl')` | Unacknowledged rows survive restart/overflow/error. Gaps block ACK and the existing direct identity rewrite; only a settled successful cycle appends ACK. |
| Plan/review authority | Exact task-artifact/observability wave bodies, evidence selectors, reviewer route/thread receipts, bounded review hot index | Review status, latest wave, obligations, compact findings | Exact artifact/source handle plus SHA/range/thread selectors | Missing or partial evidence is `DEGRADED`/`NOT_RUN`, never PASS. Exact artifacts remain bound to the reviewed candidate SHA; hot indexes may rotate only after the source is retained. |
| Canonical versus execution roots | Canonical budget/data root owns identity, authority, biography, results, promoted observability; execution drives own tools, workspace, transient trajectory | Project/fork/task lenses and status projections | Existing canonical-root resolver, task-result pointers, source handles | A fork is an execution lens, not a second mind. Copy-back/promotion precedes GC for anything referenced by a canonical result; missing legacy bytes become an explicit gap. |
## 11. Frozen Contracts v1 (`ouroboros/contracts/`)

Phase 1 of the three-layer refactor introduces a minimal, **frozen** ABI the skill/extension layer will rely on. The package lives in `ouroboros/contracts/` and is deliberately small — it declares structural contracts only, not new runtime behaviour. Existing code is not required to import from it; the protocols are verified against the real implementations via `tests/test_contracts.py`.

### 11.1 What is frozen

The browser-envelope ABI includes `ClaudexorLoginJobResponse` (required top-level `job`, operation metadata beside it) and `ClaudexorLoginJobProblem` (required `error`, optional `code` and bounded `required_actions`). Their active owner is `ouroboros/gateway/contracts.py`, mirrored by `web/modules/api_types.js` and pinned by the contract/parity suites; an additive browser gateway ABI, not a new file in the `ouroboros/contracts/` package.

| `PluginAPI` (Phase 4, v1.4) + `ExtensionRegistrationError` + `FORBIDDEN_EXTENSION_SETTINGS` + `VALID_EXTENSION_PERMISSIONS` + `VALID_EXTENSION_ROUTE_METHODS` — the surface every `type: extension` skill's `plugin.py::register(api)` binds against (`register_tool`, `register_route`, `register_ws_handler`, `register_ui_tab`, `register_settings_section`, `register_supervised_task`, `register_companion_process`, `subscribe_event`, `get_skill_token`, `send_ws_message`, `on_unload`, `log`, `get_settings`, `get_state_dir`, `skill_job_dir`, `get_runtime_info`); `skill_job_dir(job_id)` creates isolated `jobs/<sanitized_id>-<hash>/{assets,output,tmp}` folders; `VALID_EXTENSION_PERMISSIONS` includes host-mediated permissions (`companion_process`, `supervised_task`, `subscribe_event`, `inject_chat`, `presence`) requiring review/owner grants; `ExecutionMode` capability matrix (`MATRIX_CAPABILITIES`/`OUT_OF_PROCESS_UNAVAILABLE_CAPABILITIES`/`capability_available`/`available_capabilities`) is the SSOT | `ouroboros/contracts/plugin_api.py` | `tests/test_contracts.py::test_plugin_api_surface_is_frozen` pins the frozen method set; `::test_extension_route_methods_contract_matches_server_dispatch` pins the route-methods tuple; `tests/test_extension_loader.py::test_plugin_api_impl_matches_protocol` asserts `PluginAPIImpl` structurally satisfies the Protocol |
| `SkillManifest` — unified `SKILL.md` / `skill.json` format (`type: instruction \| script \| extension`; reviewed `scheduled_tasks` cron metadata; optional bounded canonical `conflicts` names; forward-compatible `presence:` block parsed by `presence_profile.py`) | `ouroboros/contracts/skill_manifest.py` | `parse_skill_manifest_text()` tolerates missing optional fields; `validate()` returns warnings without raising; Presence-profile tests pin strict nested schema |
| `schema_versions` — opt-in `_schema_version` key + `with_schema_version`/`read_schema_version` helpers | `ouroboros/contracts/schema_versions.py` | First wired by the extension `health.json` vector; other legacy state files still read as version 0 until migrated |

### 11.2 What is NOT frozen (intentionally)

- The full `ToolContext` dataclass (browser state, review history, model overrides, …) remains mutable implementation detail.
- `OUROBOROS_SCHEMA_VERSION` of `state.json` / `queue_snapshot.json` / `task_results/*.json` is treated as `0` (legacy) until the helpers are wired in.
- The raw WebSocket/HTTP *values* — only the *shape keys* are pinned.
- The `SKILL.md` body (human-readable markdown) — only the frontmatter schema is pinned.

### 11.3 What to do when extending

Any extension of the ABI MUST:

1. Add the new field/envelope key to its active frozen owner: `ouroboros/contracts/` for package protocols, or `ouroboros/gateway/contracts.py` plus `web/modules/api_types.js` for the browser gateway ABI.
2. Mention the new frozen surface here (Section 11.1 table).
3. Update `tests/test_contracts.py` so the new surface is enforced.

Removing anything from Section 11.1 is a deliberate ABI break and requires a version bump + a migration note in the release row.

### 11.4 Recent ABI Retirements

- Retired the native skill upgrade migration banner API (`GET /api/migrations`, `POST /api/migrations/{key}/dismiss`, and `MigrationsResponse`). The release row is the migration note: old dismissed banner state in `data/state/migrations.json` is intentionally ignored by current runtimes.
## 12. Host Service, Companion Processes, and Chat IDs

Host Service is a loopback, authenticated callback boundary for reviewed skills, not a general owner API. Its opaque token is bound to the skill's content hash, executable review, enablement, and grants; secrets never enter the token. Reviewed transports may inject attributed owner messages, progress, and extension events through this boundary. External slash commands bind a separate positive-identity owner chat slot from the local web owner, so an unidentified transport cannot bind or execute commands and opening the desktop first cannot lock out an approved remote owner.

The frozen route family exposes identity and tool schemas, attributed chat injection and internal-chat allocation, Presence ingress and late-work lookup, an extension WebSocket-out bridge, and the event stream. Each request consumes the token at construction time; extension permissions still decide which route exists for that skill. An owner-bound reviewed transport is a first-class control surface, including slash commands, because review evaluates identity binding, attribution, polling bounds, panic cleanup, token confinement, and exfiltration rather than imposing a screen-only product policy.

`POST /presence/turn` requires the content-hash-bound `presence` permission and accepts one opaque `binding_id`, one exact attributed event, and optional files already staged beneath the calling skill's state root. The host resolves the owner-created binding from `state/presence_bindings.json`, verifies the authenticated transport and exact provider/account/conversation/thread origin, admits a fresh reviewed behavior snapshot, and runs a fresh bounded agent. Cross-process file locks enforce the installation-wide cap and serialize one `conversation_key`; each transport's durable custody owns provider-arrival FIFO before Host admission. A stable event-derived task id makes transport retries idempotent. Input and output join the ordinary dialogue history with exact transport, actor, conversation, message, and source-event provenance rather than a second memory system.

The turn completes with one typed outcome: `message`, `silent`, `tool_delivered`, or `deferred`. `deferred` is accepted only when promotion produced a correlated `work_ref`; `GET /presence/work/{work_ref}?binding_id=...` lets the same authenticated transport poll that bound late result without exposing the general task API. Promotion clears any requested Project, workspace, or source widening, while promotion and either one-shot or recurring `schedule_followup` carry the same positive capability ceiling and Presence return context into ordinary supervised work. An admitted turn may use `presence_cancel_work` only for a `work_ref` whose stored binding and conversation match the current turn. Owner chat and Background Consciousness may call `initiate_presence` on an existing binding; that creates the same reviewed bounded cycle and counts delivery only when the cycle uses a selected transport tool and finishes `tool_delivered`.

Companion processes are host-supervised skill children. Their reviewed descriptors enter durable process custody, are reconciled by the server after lifecycle changes or restart, and stop on disable, unload, or panic. Worker-side changes write durable reconcile requests rather than spawning server-owned children. `contracts/chat_id_policy.py` keeps human-visible and synthetic IDs distinct; `chat_id=0` belongs to the Skill Review panel and is a REAL destination — membership routing (`message_bus.notification_chat_route`) delivers its frames (a subagent's terminal evidence frame included), history replay renders them in the Main stream like every other chat-0 progress frame, and negative (A2A) ids never enter a human stream.

In-process and isolated-dependency extensions use the same companion catalog. Cataloging records the manifest-declared descriptor; only the server instantiates it through `extension_companion.py` and snapshots the live projection in `state/extension_companions.json`. Durable markers under `state/extension_reconcile/` bridge worker-side enable/review changes back to that single owner. When a failing companion exhausts its restart budget, the terminal reason is persisted in the skill's existing health state before it disappears from the live snapshot, so the Skills surface continues to show an actionable load error; a later successful start clears only that matching failure. Companion cwd is the reviewed payload directory, so any payload edit stales review before reload instead of silently changing a live process.

`contracts/plugin_api.py` is the frozen extension ABI for tools, routes, WebSocket handlers, UI/settings surfaces, supervised work, companions, events, runtime facts, state roots, skill tokens, and the `presence` Host Service permission. It exposes only declared reviewed capability. Core and custom secrets remain owner state and are resolved for a skill only after fresh review and explicit grant.

## 13. External Skills Layer

Native bundled payloads and editable external payloads live in separate data-plane buckets; review, owner grants, enablement, dependency state, tokens, and health live under `data/state/skills/<name>/`. Discovery and manifest parsing establish identity, source, content hash, provenance, and declared conflicts — not trust. A conflict declared by either enabled peer is enforced symmetrically without deleting either payload.

The executable sequence is install or update → deterministic preflight → content-hash-bound review → owner grants → isolated dependency readiness → enablement → execution. These are independent gates: PASS does not install dependencies, `enabled=true` does not imply readiness, and an extension surface must also pass host registration. Mutating lifecycle work runs through one deduplicated queue; review jobs retain exact task, source, hash, attempt, actor, and terminal evidence, while compact UI history remains a projection of the private full record.

Review ordinals are allocated only after a job starts under the lifecycle lock. A started failure, cancellation, or timeout consumes its number and writes one idempotent terminal row; a pre-start dedupe consumes none. This makes retry history explainable across hash changes without letting a UI counter become review authority. Accepted rebuttals reduce repeated reviewer thrash, but a new payload hash still requires fresh evidence.

Skill review combines deterministic preflight with the authoritative multi-model checklist review; an optional advisory remains fail-open and cannot replace it. Official catalog payloads receive their narrow reduced-noise profile only when the sidecar, catalog file set, local runtime-reachable file set, and every SHA-256 match exactly. That profile may soften previously reviewed hygiene findings, but manifest, permissions, repository mutation, path, environment, chat injection, event, and token boundaries remain hard; a deterministic preflight failure is non-executable in every enforcement mode.

`skills/telegram/` is the bundled owner transport: the in-process extension owns binding, polling, injection, outbound events and settings, while a supervised companion owns the optional authenticated sidecar, tunnel, menu rollback, heartbeat, and singleton lifecycle. The text bridge remains usable when the optional Mini App or tunnel is unavailable. Its payload is seeded with hash-bound native provenance but remains disabled until required token and host-permission grants are approved; it is not installed from a marketplace or reclassified as an ordinary repair target. Telegram owner settings and binding, generated companion config/status, menu rollback snapshot, delivery cursors, and the verified tunnel cache are separate state classes under `data/state/skills/telegram/`; runtime projections and locks never replace durable owner or rollback authority. Launcher seeding supplies reviewed bytes; Colab waits for native discovery and a fresh executable seed projection, grants only missing grantable items when owner auto-grant policy permits, and then enables the requested surfaces.

Marketplace payloads install into the data plane with provenance and per-file hash checks, then enter the same lifecycle. Archives are bounded, staged privately, and landed atomically; install metadata drives isolated dependencies, while manual instructions remain guidance rather than executable metadata.

Extension registration separates reviewed dependency-free in-process code from isolated-dependency child dispatch. `_stage_extension_import_tree` creates owner-scoped staged trees so concurrent workers cannot remove a peer's live import while stale trees remain reclaimable. Namespace and permission contracts, environment scrubbing, timeout and output bounds, atomic unload, and companion cleanup preserve host liveness. Per-call children may proxy declared tools, routes, WebSocket/UI/settings surfaces, and companion descriptors; persistent event subscriptions and supervised tasks require an in-process or companion lifecycle. This distinction is reported through generic capabilities rather than inferred from a platform name.

The isolated child runs the same staged loader inside a private base and receives an explicit execution-mode capability matrix. Its environment is scrubbed, native crashes do not take down `server.py`, and every tool/route/WebSocket dispatch has bounded input, output, and lifetime. In-process extensions remain more powerful, so namespacing, declared permissions, per-skill registration tracking, and atomic unload are part of their executable contract rather than optional hygiene.

Transport metadata records source and session generically. Repair must enqueue a real managed `skill_repair` task with payload confinement and review authority: an ephemeral read-only turn cannot mutate or review the payload it was asked to fix. The routing fix belongs at task admission, not in looser editor guards.
